- **Problem:** The single most common write action (New Lead) opens a modal that looks like a different product — it ignores theming/dark-mode variables, so in light mode it is a jarring black box, and its font/spacing/border radii do not match the sibling modals. This is the clearest 'pile of modules' tell in the capture flow.
- **Recommendation:** Rewrite AddLeadModal to the same CSS-variable inline-style pattern as BulkAssignModal (same backdrop, r-modal class, var(--surface)/var(--text)/var(--border), gradient button). No behavior change needed.

**`design-system-1` — Lead-status color map is independently redefined in 6 files with divergent values — the same lead looks different on every screen**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · consistency · _Design System &amp; Visual Consistency_  
- **Evidence:** The exact same six lead statuses are hardcoded separately in: leads-list/page.js:24 STATUS_META (New text:#4338ca, bg rgba(99,102,241,0.12)), dashboard/page.js:35 STATUS_COLORS (New text:#818cf8, bg ...0.15), reports/page.js:24 STATUS_COLORS (New flat '#6366f1', no bg/text shape at all), leads/[id]/page.js:103 STATUS_META, AICommandCenter.js:17 STATUS_COLORS. The text colors deliberately differ (dark #4338ca for light chips vs light #818cf8 for dark chips) and the object SHAPE differs ({dot,bg,text} vs a bare hex string), so they cannot be swapped. A lead in 'Negotiating' is orange-on-light in the list but orange-on-dark in the dashboard, and a flat dot in reports.
- **Problem:** There is no single source of truth for what a lead status looks like. Each of the 6 surfaces a user moves between (dashboard → list → lead detail → reports → AI command center) renders the identical status with a slightly different color/shape, which reads as five different products rather than one. It is also a maintenance trap: adding/renaming a status (already a real concern given the pricing/CRM churn in memory) requires editing 6 files in 6 shapes.
- **Recommendation:** Add a single lib/leadStatus.js exporting LEAD_STATUSES = [{id,label,dot,bg,text}] (keep the existing dot hues, which already agree) plus a tiny <StatusBadge status=.. /> that reads it. Migrate the 6 call-sites to import it. Backward-compatible (same hues), additive, no rewrite. Start with leads-list + dashboard (highest traffic) then the rest.

**`design-system-2` — No shared Badge/Pill primitive — status pills have 5 incompatible implementations and a different tone palette per module**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · consistency · _Design System &amp; Visual Consistency_  
- **Evidence:** Distinct pill implementations: invoices/page.js:15 STATUS_COLORS{bg,text,dot,label}; contracts/page.js:23 const Pill=({s}) reading its own STATUS map (fontWeight 800, radius 999, uppercase); control/ControlShell.js:135 export function Pill({tone}) with a completely separate palette (green '#34d399', amber '#fbbf24', red '#f87171', blue, purple); leads-list STATUS_META dot+chip. Meanwhile globals.css:95 defines a .badge class that is used 0 times across the 100 component/page files (grep className="badge" → 0). Contracts and Control both name their component 'Pill' but they take different props (s vs tone) and different colors.
- **Problem:** Badges are the most repeated atom in a CRM/SaaS and right now every module ships its own — different radii (999 everywhere but font-weight 700 vs 800, padding 2px 9px vs 3px 9px vs 3px 10px), different semantic palettes (Control's 'green' #34d399 vs invoices 'paid' #10b981/#047857), and the design-system .badge nobody uses. This is the single clearest 'pile of modules' signal in the UI.
- **Recommendation:** Create components/ui/Badge.js (token-driven: props label, tone='neutral|success|warning|danger|info|accent', size). Define the tone→color map ONCE using the existing --success/--warning/--danger/--accent tokens. Replace contracts Pill, ControlShell Pill, invoices status chip, and the dead .badge usages. Keep STATUS maps only as data that feeds tone, not styling.

**`design-system-3` — 17 modals hand-roll the overlay/backdrop/escape logic; no shared Modal — and AddLeadModal/confirm.js diverge from the inline-token convention entirely**  
HIGH · L effort · high impact · consistency · _Design System &amp; Visual Consistency_  
- **Evidence:** 17 files contain a hand-built fixed overlay (grep position:'fixed',inset:0 + backdrop / 'fixed inset-0' → 17). They disagree fundamentally: AddLeadModal.js:41-47 uses raw Tailwind with HARDCODED hex (bg-[#0f1117], border-white/10, from-violet-600 to-cyan-600) ignoring the var(--*) tokens; confirm.js:121-240 ships a scoped <style> block with its OWN hardcoded hex (#14161f, gradient #6366f1→#a855f7) and a THIRD button palette; ScheduleMeetingModal/PlanWelcomeModal use var(--*) inline; Studio uses .ms-modal (studio.css:324). Backdrops range from bg-black/60 to rgba(0,0,0,0.6) to rgba(6,6,8,0.6); radii 16/16/var(--ms-radius-modal).
- **Problem:** Every dialog re-implements escape-to-close, click-outside, scroll-lock, focus, and animation, and they look different module to module. AddLeadModal in particular won't follow the light/dark theme at all (hardcoded #0f1117) and uses brand colors (violet→cyan) that appear nowhere else in Core. This is duplicated logic AND duplicated, drifting visuals.
- **Recommendation:** Extract components/ui/Modal.js (overlay + card + header/title/close + footer slot, escape/click-out/scroll-lock built in, styled with var(--surface)/--border/--radius). Migrate Core modals first (AddLeadModal, ScheduleMeetingModal, the 17 ad-hoc ones); leave Studio's .ms-modal as the intentional themed exception. Fold confirm.js's ConfirmDialog onto the same Modal so there's one dialog chrome.

**`design-system-4` — Shared confirm() dialog exists but is under-adopted — 6+ surfaces still fire native window.confirm/alert, including public client-facing pages**  
HIGH · S effort · medium impact · ux · _Design System &amp; Visual Consistency_  
- **Evidence:** lib/confirm.js provides a polished useConfirm() used in only 10 files. Still calling the raw browser dialog: contracts/page.js:116 (window.confirm(`Delete "${d.title}"?`)), leads-list/page.js, studio/[id]/page.js, studio/trash/page.js, contracts/[id]/page.js, and the PUBLIC token page booking/manage/[token]/page.js (grep window.confirm|window.alert → 6 files). The public booking surface dropping to an OS-native gray confirm box is especially jarring.
- **Problem:** Destructive actions (delete contract, delete photo, cancel booking) inconsistently use either the branded dialog or the ugly native one depending on which file the dev was in. On public client-facing pages it breaks the premium impression the studio is selling. It's also a half-migrated primitive — the worst state, because it looks like the inconsistency is accidental.
- **Recommendation:** This is the cheapest high-value win: replace the 6 remaining window.confirm/alert calls with the existing useConfirm() (already imported app-wide via ConfirmProvider). For public token pages that may render outside the provider, the fallback in confirm.js:69 already handles it — but better to wrap those layouts in the provider so they get the styled dialog too.

**`contracts-7` — Builder toolbar relies on bare icons (incl. a literal “¶”) with only hover tooltips**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · discoverability · standard: Feature Discovery · _Contracts Studio_  
- **Evidence:** Ten toolbar buttons in [id]/page.js:85-93; several are icon-only with title= tooltips, including the clause inserter rendered as the literal pilcrow character "¶" (line 89), and People/Room/Versions/Settings as bare lucide icons.
- **Problem:** A first-time user cannot tell what these do without hovering each one, and "¶" is not a recognizable affordance for "insert a saved clause". On touch devices there are no hover tooltips at all, so the features are effectively invisible. This hurts discoverability of genuinely valuable features (clauses, version history, team room).
- **Recommendation:** Group the secondary tools under a single labeled overflow menu ("… More") or add short text labels on wider viewports; replace the "¶" with the BookMarked/FileText icon used elsewhere and a "Clauses" label. This also de-clutters the toolbar, which currently wraps on smaller screens.

**`contracts-9` — Two divergent STATUS maps inside the same module (colors, labels, shapes differ)**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · consistency · standard: Consistency (status/badges) · _Contracts Studio_  
- **Evidence:** Overview defines STATUS as objects with label/color/bg and renders a Pill with uppercase + letter-spacing (page.js:13-23). Vault defines a DIFFERENT STATUS as [color,label] tuples with different colors (e.g. pending_approval is purple #a855f7 on Overview vs amber #f59e0b 'Needs approval' on Vault; sent is blue #3b82f6 vs indigo #6366f1) and a non-uppercase Pill (vault/page.js:9-14). A THIRD status palette (SST) exists for signers ([id]/page.js:267).
- **Problem:** The same document status renders with different color, label text, and typography depending on which screen you're on. "Approval" vs "Needs approval", different blues for "Sent" — small but exactly the kind of inconsistency that makes a product feel like stitched-together modules.
- **Recommendation:** Extract a single shared STATUS map + Pill component (e.g. in blocks.js or a contracts/_shared.js) and import it in Overview, Vault, and the signer rows. One source of truth for status color/label/shape.

**`command-center-8` — Three divergent dialog/modal patterns inside one module**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · consistency · _Command Center (internal platform control plane — /control + /api/cc/*)_  
- **Evidence:** support/page.js:279-301 defines a clean reusable Modal+Field component; reports/page.js:91-113 hand-rolls a separate inline modal; customers/[id], flags, plans use window.prompt() instead of any modal. storage/page.js:95 also redefines a local Stat instead of importing the shared Stat from ControlShell.js:126.
- **Problem:** Forms/dialogs look and behave differently page to page (overlay styles, focus, escape handling, validation), undercutting the 'one cohesive product' goal within a single module, and the shared Stat/Modal atoms are not consistently reused.
- **Recommendation:** Promote the support Modal/Field into components/control/ (alongside Card/Stat/Pill) and adopt it in reports and the customers/[id] action flows. Replace storage's local Stat with the shared one. One dialog primitive, one Stat, module-wide.

**`settings-team-6` — Role label taxonomy is inconsistent across screens (Super Admin vs Owner) with four duplicated maps**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · consistency · _Settings, Team, Onboarding & Knowledge_  
- **Evidence:** team/page.js ROLES (lines 16-21) labels super_admin 'Super Admin'. profile/page.js ROLE_LABELS (line 19) labels super_admin 'Owner'. accept-invite/page.js ROLE_LABELS (line 46) uses 'Super Admin'. settings WorkspaceTab uses a separate ROLE_INFO map. Four independent role label/color maps for the same four roles.
- **Problem:** The same role shows as 'Owner' on Profile and 'Super Admin' on Team/Invite/Workspace, and each map redefines colors independently, so they already disagree and a change must touch four files.
- **Recommendation:** Extract one shared ROLES constant (value/label/color/icon/desc) to src/lib/roles.js and import it in team, profile, accept-invite, and settings WorkspaceTab. Pick one canonical label everywhere.

**`settings-team-13` — Storage settings page is only reachable by deep link and is hardcoded dark-only**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · discoverability · _Settings, Team, Onboarding & Knowledge_  
- **Evidence:** settings/storage/page.js exists with a back button to /settings (line 45) and hardcoded dark fallbacks (#0a0a0f line 42, #14141b/#1e1e26 lines 108-110). settings/page.js TABS (lines 53-72) has no 'storage' entry and the render switch has no storage branch, so nothing in the Settings sidebar links to it.
- **Problem:** Users cannot navigate to the Storage usage page from Settings; it is an orphan route. Storage limits are plan-enforced (uploads blocked at 100%), so this is an important screen to surface - and its hardcoded dark hex ignores the light theme set in AppearanceTab.
- **Recommendation:** Add a 'Storage' tab to the Settings TABS array (or a row in Data & Privacy) linking to /settings/storage, and convert its hardcoded dark hex fallbacks to the var(--*) tokens used elsewhere so it respects light/dark theme.

**`galleries-5` — User-facing error message instructs the client to run a server command**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · ux · standard: Professional error copy · _Galleries, Albums & Portfolio (client delivery)_  
- **Evidence:** studio/[id]/page.js:416 — when a published gallery has no share_url, the Open button sets a banner reading: 'Share link not ready — the API needs a restart (pm2 restart wappflow-api), then refresh.'
- **Problem:** Leaking ops/devops instructions into the product UI is unprofessional and useless to a photographer. It also signals a real edge case (publish succeeded but share_token didn't round-trip) handled by blaming the user.
- **Recommendation:** Replace with a user-appropriate message ('Still preparing your share link — refresh in a moment') and fix the underlying cause by ensuring publishGallery's response (which returns share_url at media-studio.js:1660) is merged into gallery state, or refetch the gallery after publish so share_url is always present.

**`settings-team-11` — Toast components are reimplemented per page with divergent styling and durations**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · consistency · _Settings, Team, Onboarding & Knowledge_  
- **Evidence:** Separate Toast definitions: team/page.js line 43 (green/red rgba, top:20, 3000ms), knowledge/page.js line 36 (#111827 dark bg, 3500ms), settings/page.js line 74 (#111827, 3000ms), profile/page.js inline lines 130-144 (light bordered card, top:80, 3000ms). Team PermissionsTab also has its own 2500ms toast (line 358).
- **Problem:** The same success/error feedback looks different on every screen in this cluster (dark pill, green pill, bordered light card) with different positions and durations, undermining the 'one product' feel and multiplying maintenance.
- **Recommendation:** Extract one shared Toast/useToast into src/lib (or reuse the existing global notification util if present) and replace the local copies. Single source for color, position, icon, and duration.

**`design-system-5` — Every page hand-rolls its own input styling — 16 files define a local fld/inputStyle const; the global .input-field class is effectively unused**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · consistency · _Design System &amp; Visual Consistency_  
- **Evidence:** 16 files define a local input-style constant (grep 'const (fld|inputStyle|inp|fieldStyle)' → 16), e.g. bookings/page.js:138 const fld={padding:'8px 10px',borderRadius:8,border:'1px solid var(--border)',background:'var(--bg)',...}. globals.css:104 defines .input-field (padding 10px 14px, radius var(--radius)=10, background var(--surface2)) but pages don't use it; AddLeadModal.js:77 uses yet another Tailwind input (px-4 py-2.5, rounded-xl=12, bg-white/5, focus:border-violet-500). So inputs vary in padding (8/10/2.5×4), radius (8 vs 10 vs 12), background (var(--bg) vs var(--surface2) vs white/5) and focus color (--accent vs violet-500/cyan-500/green-500 in AddLeadModal alone).
- **Problem:** Forms are everywhere (settings, bookings, contracts, lead edit, invoices) and no two form fields are pixel-identical. AddLeadModal even uses three different focus-ring colors (violet/cyan/green) on fields in the SAME form. This is low-glamour but high-frequency inconsistency users feel on every data-entry screen.
- **Recommendation:** Add components/ui/Field.js (label + input/select/textarea) and/or a single exported inputStyle in lib (or just promote .input-field and use className). Standardize on radius var(--radius), background var(--surface2), focus border var(--accent). Migrate the 16 local fld consts to the shared one; fix AddLeadModal's three-color focus rings to one accent.

**`design-system-6` — borderRadius is ad-hoc across the app — 22 distinct pixel values in use while --radius:10px is ignored; cards range 10→18px between modules**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · consistency · _Design System &amp; Visual Consistency_  
- **Evidence:** Inline borderRadius pixel values counted across app/components: 10(255×), 8(183), 12(167), 9(130), 14(98), 20(94), 11(78), 16(64), 6(50), 18(40), 7(28), plus 3/5/4/13/24/22/2/1/38/17 — 22 distinct values. Concrete card drift: globals.css .card=var(--radius)=10; ControlShell.js:124 Card=14; bookings/page.js:57 cards=16; dashboard/page.js:33 StatCard=18; AddLeadModal rounded-2xl=16; confirm.js .cm-card=16. The --radius token exists (globals.css:24) but is rarely referenced.
- **Problem:** Corner radius is one of the strongest 'is this the same app?' cues, and WappFlow's varies by ~80% (10px to 18px) depending on which module/dev built the surface. Buttons within a single page even mix 8/9/11 (bookings copy button radius 8, save button 11, day toggle 8). It reads as visually unsettled.
- **Recommendation:** Define a small radius scale as tokens (--radius-sm:8, --radius:10, --radius-lg:14, --radius-pill:999) and apply via the new primitives (Button/Card/Field/Badge). You don't need to touch all 1000+ inline uses at once — adopting the primitives migrates the high-traffic surfaces, and a lint/codemod can sweep the rest later. Pick ONE card radius (recommend 14) and standardize Card/Stat on it.

**`design-system-7` — No EmptyState component — 30+ bespoke 'No X yet' empty states, each a one-off layout**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · consistency · _Design System &amp; Visual Consistency_  
- **Evidence:** grep 'No [a-z]+ yet' returns 30+ distinct phrasings (No leads yet, No invoices yet, No bookings yet, No clients yet, No galleries yet, No documents yet, No photos yet, No orders yet, ...). Each is hand-built: bookings/page.js:121 is a bare <p style={{fontSize:14,color:'var(--text-muted)'}}>No bookings yet…</p>; leads-list/page.js:641 is a centered <div> with a 32px icon + two <p>; Studio uses .ms-empty (studio.css:340, with display heading + CTA). So empty states range from one muted line to a full icon+heading+CTA block depending on the page.
- **Problem:** Empty states are a key first-run/onboarding moment for a maturity push, and right now they're wildly uneven — some give the user a next action (CTA), most are a dead muted sentence. There's no consistent voice, icon treatment, or call-to-action, so the product feels unfinished in exactly the moments new users hit (empty pipeline, empty gallery).
- **Recommendation:** Add components/ui/EmptyState.js (icon, title, description, optional primary CTA) styled with tokens; mirror the nice Studio .ms-empty pattern for Core. Migrate the list/index pages (leads, invoices, bookings, clients, contracts, orders) first — these are the onboarding-critical empties — and give each a real CTA (e.g. 'No bookings yet → Share your link').

**`design-system-8` — Loading states are inconsistent — 3 spinner mechanisms and ~20 distinct 'Loading…' strings, several pages just render a bare 'Loading…' line**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · consistency · _Design System &amp; Visual Consistency_  
- **Evidence:** Three spinner systems coexist: lucide <Loader2> (3 files), the .spin CSS class (globals.css:186, 3 files), and .ms-spin (studio.css:353, 7 files). ~20 distinct loading texts (Loading…, Loading leads..., Loading workspace..., Loading invoices..., Loading clients…, Loading your published work…) with inconsistent ellipsis style (… vs ...). bookings/page.js:37 renders loading as a bare <div style={{padding:40,color:'var(--text-muted)'}}>Loading…</div>; ControlShell.js:56 renders 'Loading Command Center…' centered; Studio uses .ms-loading (uppercase tracked, pulsing).
- **Problem:** Perceived speed and polish are explicit goals of the maturity push. Today the loading experience is a coin-flip between a tasteful pulsing label, a spinner, and a single muted word — and there are no skeletons anywhere for the data-heavy tables (leads-list, invoices, contracts), so those pages flash blank then pop. Inconsistent ellipsis (… vs ...) is visible drift.
- **Recommendation:** Add components/ui/Spinner.js + LoadingState.js (one mechanism, token-colored) and, for the big tables, a simple Skeleton row component. Standardize on the ellipsis character. Migrate Core full-page loaders (leads-list, invoices, dashboard, contracts, bookings) first; leave .ms-loading as Studio's intentional themed variant.

**`crm-leads-12` — MergeDuplicatesModal uses native window.confirm/window.alert, breaking the app's modal/toast conventions**  
LOW · S effort · medium impact · consistency · _CRM — Leads & Pipeline_  
- **Evidence:** leads-list/page.js:614 window.confirm with embedded \n and ⚠ emoji, line 620 window.alert on failure. The same file defines BulkTrashModal and uses showToast and useConfirm elsewhere (lines 69, 101). The bulk Move-to-Clients action also uses window.confirm (line 997).
- **Problem:** Native browser dialogs look foreign, can't be styled/dark-themed, and are inconsistent with the polished custom confirm/toast used a few lines away — exactly the inconsistency the maturity push targets.
- **Recommendation:** Replace window.confirm/alert in MergeDuplicatesModal and the Move-to-Clients bulk action with the existing useConfirm() modal and showToast(), matching BulkTrashModal.

**`design-system-9` — Heading 'bold' weight drifts across 700/800/900 with no scale; primary buttons exist in 3+ unrelated styles**  
LOW · M effort · medium impact · consistency · _Design System &amp; Visual Consistency_  
- **Evidence:** Inline fontWeight tally: 700(601×), 800(292), 600(284), 900(61) — all used for emphasis with no rule. Page H1s differ: bookings/page.js:43 h1 fontWeight 800; many cards use 700; some stat values 900 (dashboard StatCard, ControlShell Stat:130 uses 800). Primary buttons: globals.css .btn-primary (flat var(--accent), used in 4/100 files); bookings save button (var(--accent), radius 11, weight 800) at bookings/page.js:118; AddLeadModal submit is a violet→cyan gradient (AddLeadModal.js:146); confirm.js primary is a #6366f1→#a855f7 gradient (confirm.js:218); leads-list merge button is a #6366f1→#4f46e5 gradient (leads-list/page.js:670). So 'the primary button' is flat-indigo in some places and three different gradients in others.
- **Problem:** There's no typographic scale (weights and sizes are picked per-element: 13/13.5/14/14.5/15/15.5 px body sizes appear throughout), so headings and primary actions don't feel like one system. The primary CTA — the most important repeated control — has at least four visual identities (flat accent vs three gradients), which undercuts the 'one OS' goal more than any single screen.
- **Recommendation:** Define a type scale (h1/h2/h3/body/caption with fixed size+weight) as utility classes or tokens, and a components/ui/Button.js with variant='primary|secondary|ghost|danger' driven by tokens. Decide ONE primary-button treatment (recommend flat var(--accent) per existing .btn-primary, or pick one gradient and tokenize it as --accent-gradient) and migrate the gradient one-offs. This is lower severity because it's felt cumulatively rather than blocking, but it's the backbone of cohesion.

**`contracts-10` — Heavy inline-style duplication instead of shared components/CSS; module is mostly hand-rolled styles**  
LOW · L effort · medium impact · consistency · standard: Consistency / design-system reuse · _Contracts Studio_  
- **Evidence:** Every screen redefines modal chrome, buttons (btnPrimary in page.js:329, btn in settings/page.js:115, menuItem in shell), inputs (inp/miniInp), and Pills inline. The contracts.css file is only 51 lines and covers just the document canvas, not the app chrome.
- **Problem:** Buttons, modals, inputs, and toasts are re-implemented per file with slightly different padding/radius/font-size (e.g. btnPrimary padding 10px16px vs Settings btn 8px14px). This guarantees drift and makes the module inconsistent with the rest of the app's component library over time, and triples the cost of any future restyle.
- **Recommendation:** Adopt the app's shared Button/Modal/Input primitives (or promote the contracts ones into shared components) so Contracts inherits global styling. Lower priority than the integration items but it's the root cause of much of the within-module inconsistency.

**`comms-15` — Hard-coded dark sidebar ignores theme/branding tokens**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · consistency · _Communications (Team Chat + Project Rooms + Huddles/Calls + Floating WhatsApp Chat)_  
- **Evidence:** The chat sidebar uses background:'#1e1e2e' and literal #9ca3af/rgba(255,255,255,...) colors (page.js:784, 795, 835, etc.) instead of the var(--surface)/var(--text) tokens the rest of the page (and app) use. The main area correctly uses CSS vars.
- **Problem:** In light theme the sidebar stays a fixed dark slab while everything else adapts, and it ignores any workspace branding — a visible inconsistency against the 'one cohesive product' bar.
- **Recommendation:** Replace the hard-coded sidebar palette with theme tokens (or a dedicated --chat-sidebar-* token set) so it respects light/dark and future workspace branding, matching how the message area is already themed.

**`platform-15` — StudioCopilot and FloatingChat error/loading states diverge from the rest of the app**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · consistency · standard: Consistency / Loading-Error States · _Platform Plumbing (entitlements, pricing, plan UX, notifications, confirm, AI copilots, trash, WhatsApp, API client)_  
- **Evidence:** StudioCopilot.js:46-48 surfaces errors as an assistant chat bubble; FloatingChat.js:125 uses useConfirm alert for send failure; AICommandCenter.js:85-87 sets an inline error result block; studio/trash uses a bespoke toast (page.js:19). Four different error presentations across this one layer.
- **Problem:** Error and feedback affordances are ad hoc per component (chat bubble vs modal alert vs inline card vs toast), so the platform lacks a consistent 'something went wrong' language — a polish gap users feel as inconsistency.
- **Recommendation:** Standardize on one toast/inline pattern for non-blocking failures (and reserve confirm() for blocking decisions). A shared useToast() alongside useConfirm would unify FloatingChat, StudioCopilot, studio/trash, and command center.

**`studio-library-10` — Watermark publish/restart leak: gallery 'Open' falls back to a developer-facing pm2 restart message**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · consistency · _Media Studio — Library, Cull & AI_  
- **Evidence:** studio/[id]/page.js:416 — when a published gallery lacks share_url the Open button sets banner 'Share link not ready — the API needs a restart (pm2 restart wappflow-api), then refresh.'
- **Problem:** End-user UI exposes an internal ops instruction (pm2 restart) that a photographer can neither understand nor perform. It signals an unfinished edge case and breaks the polished, control-first tone of the rest of Studio.
- **Recommendation:** Replace with a user-appropriate message ('Still finalising your link — refresh in a moment') and, better, have publish always return share_url (it does at :1660) so this branch is unreachable; if a legacy published gallery truly lacks a token, re-issue one server-side on Open rather than asking for a restart.

### Fragmented navigation & four disconnected shells

**`navigation-ia-3` — Lead/Client detail page highlights no nav item and its Back button always says "Dashboard" regardless of where you came from**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · ux · _Navigation & Information Architecture_  
- **Evidence:** NavBar.js:331-332 isActive uses pathname.startsWith(path); the Leads nav item path is '/leads-list' (NavBar.js:169) while the detail route is '/leads/[id]' — startsWith('/leads-list') does NOT match '/leads/123' and neither does startsWith('/dashboard'), so on a lead detail page NO top-nav item is highlighted. Meanwhile leads/[id]/page.js:1203-1205 and 1291-1293 hard-code the back control to router.push('/dashboard') with the label 'Back to Dashboard' / 'Dashboard'. Entry points to this page are /leads-list (leads-list/page.js:945,1150), /dashboard kanban (dashboard/page.js:1252,1270), AND /clients (clients/page.js:129-130, which opens a client via /leads/${c.id}).
- **Problem:** Three different lists feed into the lead detail screen, but Back always dumps the user on Dashboard — so a user browsing the Leads list or the Clients grid, opening a record, then hitting Back is teleported to a screen they never came from and has to re-navigate and re-scroll. Simultaneously the nav bar shows no active state, so they can't even orient by the nav. This is a daily-friction 'Where am I / how do I get back' failure on the app's most-visited detail screen.
- **Recommendation:** Two fixes: (1) In NavBar isActive, treat '/leads/' as belonging to the Leads tab (e.g. special-case so the Leads item is active for both /leads-list and /leads/[id]). (2) Replace the hard-coded '/dashboard' back target in leads/[id]/page.js with router.back() (falling back to /leads-list), or pass the origin via a query param, so Back returns to the actual list. For client records opened from /clients, return to /clients.

**`contracts-1` — Contracts Studio opens in a separate browser tab with its own shell, fracturing the one-OS experience**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · integration · standard: Integration / consistent navigation · _Contracts Studio_  
- **Evidence:** AppSwitcher.js:58 launches Contracts Studio with target:'_blank' ({ href: '/contracts', target: '_blank' }); ContractsStudioShell.js is a fully separate header/nav (its own AppSwitcher, account menu, sign-out) distinct from the CRM NavBar used everywhere else.
- **Problem:** A user working a deal in the CRM clicks Contracts Studio and is thrown into a NEW OS-level browser tab with a different chrome, different nav, and a duplicated account menu. Coming back to the lead means hunting for the original tab. The two shells also drift visually/behaviorally over time. This is the single biggest contributor to Contracts feeling like a bolted-on module rather than part of WappFlow.
- **Recommendation:** Keep the dedicated shell (Media-Studio pattern is fine) but stop forcing target:'_blank' — navigate in the same tab like every other module, and add a persistent way back to the linked lead/CRM from the builder. At minimum, when a document has a lead_id, show a "Back to {client}" link in the builder toolbar that routes to /leads/{id}.
- _Reviewer nuance:_ Confirmed: AppSwitcher.js:58 launches Contracts Studio with target:'_blank'; ContractsStudioShell.js is a fully separate sticky header (line 38) with its own AppSwitcher (39), account menu, and sign-out (63), distinct from the CRM NavBar. NUANCE: the same target:'_blank' new-tab pattern is used for Media Studio at AppSwitcher.js:57 (and Flux at 59) — so target:'_blank' is the established cross-module convention, NOT a Contracts-specific defect. Calling it 'the single biggest contributor to Contracts feeling bolted-on' overstates it, since Media Studio behaves identically yet isn't flagged. The real, defensible part is the absence of a 'Back to {client}/lead' affordance in the builder when lead_id is set (rec's minimum). Keep the rec, drop the 'single biggest contributor' framing and the implication it's unique to Contracts.

**`navigation-ia-1` — Module switching opens new browser tabs instead of navigating in-app, fracturing the "one product" feel**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · ux · _Navigation & Information Architecture_  
- **Evidence:** AppSwitcher.js:57-58 opens Media Studio and Contracts with target:'_blank' ({ href:'/studio', target:'_blank' } and { href:'/contracts', target:'_blank' }); only WappFlow CRM (line 56) navigates in-place. StudioShell.js:64-75 mirrors this in reverse — its waffle menu opens /dashboard and /contracts with target="_blank". layout.js:46-57 contains a bespoke session-inheritance script precisely BECAUSE new tabs would otherwise drop the session. So the canonical way to move between the four modules is to spawn a new tab each time.
- **Problem:** A user who works across CRM, Studio and Contracts in a session accumulates a pile of duplicate tabs, loses the SPA history stack (browser Back no longer returns them to where they were inside the app), and gets a full cold-load of the next module's bundle every switch. This is the strongest signal that WappFlow is four apps, not one operating system — the exact opposite of the stated product direction.
- **Recommendation:** Make every entry in AppSwitcher and the in-shell switchers navigate in the same tab via router.push (drop target:'_blank' on lines 57-58 of AppSwitcher.js and lines 64,72,83 of StudioShell.js). They are all same-origin Next routes, so client-side navigation keeps the session, the back-stack, and shared chunks warm. Keep new-tab behavior only for genuinely external Flux (FLUX_URL). This single change makes switching feel instant and unified.

**`navigation-ia-5` — Four divergent shells with different chrome and a per-page wrapping model instead of layout.js**  
HIGH · L effort · high impact · architecture · _Navigation & Information Architecture_  
- **Evidence:** Only /control mounts its shell via a real layout (control/layout.js:7-11 wraps children in ControlShell). CRM, Studio and Contracts mount the shell INSIDE every page: dashboard/page.js:23 imports NavBar and wraps render in <NavBar> (887/1345); studio/page.js:8 imports StudioShell aliased as NavBar (134/215); contracts/page.js:10 wraps <ContractsStudioShell> (62/148); studio/layout.js:9-16 and contracts/layout.js:5-7 only set the tab title and CSS, they do NOT mount the shell. The shells themselves diverge: NavBar is a 60px fixed top bar (NavBar.js:398-403), ControlShell is a 232px left sidebar (ControlShell.js:64), StudioShell uses a separate --ms-* token namespace, and each re-implements its own user dropdown + outside-click handler.
- **Problem:** Because the shell is re-rendered per page rather than persisted by a layout, switching pages within a module fully unmounts/remounts the nav (losing dropdown/scroll state and flashing), and switching modules swaps the entire chrome. Four hand-maintained shells mean fixes (e.g. a new nav item, an accessibility fix) must be applied four times and drift apart — they already have. This structural split is why the product feels like a pile of modules.
- **Recommendation:** Without a rewrite: progressively hoist each module's shell into its route-group layout.js (move <NavBar> into a (crm) layout, <StudioShell> into studio/layout.js, <ContractsStudioShell> into contracts/layout.js) so the shell persists across in-module navigation. Then unify the three top-bar shells behind one <AppShell variant="crm|studio|contracts"> that shares the logo, AppSwitcher, user menu and notification bell, with module-specific nav passed as a prop. Studio keeps its --ms-* theme via the variant. This converges the chrome while respecting Studio's intentional creative skin.

**`integration-4` — CRM, Contracts Studio, and Media Studio are three separate nav shells with no in-context jump**  
MED · L effort · high impact · integration · _integration_  
- **Evidence:** NavBar.js:167-176 CRM nav = Dashboard/Leads/Clients/Inbox/Invoices/Bookings/Analytics; Contracts and Studio are absent — they open in their own shells via AppSwitcher (StudioShell.js, ContractsStudioShell.js) and even set their own document.title (NavBar.js:207-210). ms_projects.lead_id and cs_documents.lead_id exist but the shells don't cross-link back to the lead.
- **Problem:** The end-to-end job (lead -> contract -> shoot -> gallery -> invoice) crosses three full-page context switches with different navigation and styling, and once inside Studio or Contracts there's no breadcrumb back to the originating lead. It feels like three apps sharing a login, not one OS — exactly the perception the maturity push wants to fix.
- **Recommendation:** Add a persistent 'client context' breadcrumb/back-link inside Studio and Contracts shells when a lead_id is in scope (project.lead_id / doc.lead_id), linking to /leads/[id]. Optionally let the AppSwitcher carry the current lead so switching modules keeps the client in focus. No data-model change required.

**`navigation-ia-2` — Command Center (/control) is a navigational dead-end — no link back to the product**  
HIGH · S effort · medium impact · ux · _Navigation & Information Architecture_  
- **Evidence:** control/ControlShell.js renders a self-contained sidebar (lines 64-84) + topbar (lines 89-115) whose only controls are the search box, the admin name, and a Sign-out button (line 113). There is no AppSwitcher, no logo-links-to-dashboard, and no 'Back to app' control. A repo-wide grep for /dashboard, AppSwitcher, /studio, /contracts under app/control + components/control returns only one hit (customers/[id]/page.js:106, which is body copy, not a link). The shell also uses a totally different visual language (left sidebar, hardcoded fallback colors var(--bg,#0a0a0f)) from the other three top-bar shells.
- **Problem:** An operator who lands in Command Center can only leave by editing the URL or using browser Back. Fails 'Where am I / what can I do next' — there is no visible relationship between Command Center and the product it controls. It reads as a bolted-on internal tool, undermining the cohesive-OS goal.
- **Recommendation:** Add the WappFlow logo (linking to /dashboard) at the top of the ControlShell sidebar and an 'Exit to app' item in the topbar account area. Since Command Center is platform-admin only, you don't need the full AppSwitcher — just one obvious escape hatch — but reusing the existing logo+wordmark block from NavBar keeps it on-brand.

**`navigation-ia-6` — Media Studio top nav never shows an active state (the CSS class exists but is never applied)**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · consistency · _Navigation & Information Architecture_  
- **Evidence:** StudioShell.js:99-100 renders the two primary nav buttons with a static class only: <button className="ms-shell-link" onClick={() => router.push('/studio')}>Shoots</button> and the same for Portfolio — no conditional .is-active. Yet studio.css:200 defines .ms-shell-link.is-active { background: var(--ms-surface-2); color: var(--ms-ink); }. The active style is fully built and simply never wired up. By contrast ContractsStudioShell.js:25-33 DOES compute active = pathname === href and style accordingly, and ControlShell.js:59 computes isActive too — so Studio is the inconsistent one.
- **Problem:** Inside Media Studio you cannot tell whether you're on Shoots or Portfolio from the nav — both look identical at all times. Direct 'Where am I?' failure, made worse by the inconsistency that the other two shells get this right.
- **Recommendation:** In StudioShell, read usePathname() and add className={pathname === '/studio' ? 'ms-shell-link is-active' : 'ms-shell-link'} (and the equivalent startsWith('/studio/portfolio') for Portfolio). Zero new CSS needed — the .is-active rule already exists.

**`navigation-ia-7` — Inconsistent, label-less back navigation across Studio sub-pages (no breadcrumbs)**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · consistency · _Navigation & Information Architecture_  
- **Evidence:** Each Studio detail screen rolls its own back affordance with a different label and style: studio/[id]/page.js:346 'All shoots'; studio/[id]/cull/page.js:452 'Back to shoot' and :498 the shoot title; studio/[id]/video/page.js:72 just 'Back'; studio/[id]/album/[albumId]/page.js:53 'Shoot'; studio/[id]/reel/[reelId]/page.js:48 'Shoot'. The video page back button says only 'Back' with no destination context. There is no breadcrumb (Studio › Shoot › Cull) anywhere, even though these are 2-3 levels deep.
- **Problem:** A user deep in cull/video/album/reel sees a different back-control label and shape on every screen, and on the video page can't tell where 'Back' goes. Without breadcrumbs the depth of the current location is invisible. Fails 'Where am I?' and adds per-screen cognitive load in the module that goes deepest.
- **Recommendation:** Add one shared <StudioBreadcrumb> component (Studio › {shoot title} › {Cull|Video|Album|Reel}) rendered at the top of every studio/[id]/* page, replacing the ad-hoc back buttons. Each crumb routes to its level. Reuse the existing ms-back / ms-btn-ghost styles so it matches the theme.

**`navigation-ia-8` — Settings, Help and Trash are fragmented across modules with no shared pattern or cross-links**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · consistency · _Navigation & Information Architecture_  
- **Evidence:** Settings live at four unrelated routes: /settings (settings/page.js, 2946 lines, tab-based), /settings/storage, /studio/settings, /contracts/settings. Help is triplicated: /help, /studio/help, /contracts/help. Trash exists at /trash AND /studio/trash. Each module's user dropdown only links to its own settings/help (StudioShell.js:125-127 → /studio/settings, /studio/help, /studio/trash; ContractsStudioShell.js:60-61 → /contracts/settings, /contracts/help; NavBar.js:554-585 → /settings + /help). None links to the others, and the global /settings page (a 2946-line tabbed monster) doesn't surface module settings.
- **Problem:** A user looking for 'settings' or 'trash' has to first figure out which module owns the thing they want to configure, and there's no single place that lists them. The same conceptual surface (Settings/Help/Trash) is implemented as separate, non-discoverable islands — the hallmark of a module pile rather than an OS.
- **Recommendation:** Don't merge the screens (they're legitimately module-scoped), but make them discoverable from one place: add a Settings index (or a section in the existing /settings tabs) that links out to Studio settings, Contracts settings and Storage; and unify Help as one /help hub with module sub-sections. Use a consistent route convention ({module}/settings, {module}/help, {module}/trash) and surface a shared 'Settings' affordance in every shell's account menu.

**`navigation-ia-9` — The "Bookings" nav item lands on a configuration screen, not the bookings it implies**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · discoverability · _Navigation & Information Architecture_  
- **Evidence:** NavBar.js:173 adds a top-level nav item { label:'Bookings', path:'/bookings' }. bookings/page.js:43 titles the page 'Bookings' but the body (lines 46-89) is entirely setup: the public booking link to copy (48-55), an editable Services list (57-71), and Weekly availability toggles (73-89). The subhead (line 46) confirms it: 'Let clients self-book…'. There is no list of who has actually booked — those arrive as leads/reminders elsewhere.
- **Problem:** A primary nav label promises a list of bookings but delivers a settings form. Users clicking 'Bookings' to see today's appointments hit a config page and leave confused — a 'What can I do here?' mismatch on a top-level destination.
- **Recommendation:** Either rename the nav item/page to 'Booking page' / 'Booking setup', or (better, additive) split the screen into two tabs — 'Upcoming' (the actual booked appointments, sourced from the leads/reminders the bookings create) and 'Setup' (the current services + availability form). Keep the route at /bookings.

**`crm-leads-7` — 11 top-level tabs on the detail page is overwhelming and inconsistently styled (emoji-prefixed labels)**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · ux · _CRM — Leads & Pipeline_  
- **Evidence:** leads/[id]/page.js:1214-1226 — TABS = Timeline, Notes, Reminders, History, Invoices, Emails, Email Flow, Related, '💬 Team Room', '✨ AI Assistant', '🏭 Industry AI'. Three labels embed emoji while eight do not; counts are shown for some, hard-coded 0 for Timeline/Room/AI.
- **Problem:** A horizontally-scrolling 11-tab strip with mixed emoji/text labels is hard to scan and signals features bolted on over time. The hard-coded count:0 on Timeline also misleads.
- **Recommendation:** Group tabs: a primary row (Activity, Notes, Reminders, Invoices, Emails) and an overflow 'More ▾' menu (Email Flow, Related, Team Room, AI, Industry AI). Drop emoji from labels and use the existing lucide icons consistently. Remove the fake count:0 badges.

**`client-portal-7` — No navigation between client surfaces; the only cross-link opens a new tab inconsistently**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · consistency · standard: Cross-surface navigation · _Client Portal & the public client journey (client/[token], g/[token], d/[token], shop/[token], pay/[token], book/[slug], booking/manage/[token], folio/[handle])_  
- **Evidence:** The only inter-surface link is the gallery's 'Order prints' → /shop (g/[token]/page.js:203) which uses target=_blank, while the portal's gallery/doc rows also open _blank (client/[token]/page.js:50). There is no shared header/footer linking the surfaces, and no path from shop/pay/book/folio back to the portal.
- **Problem:** A client landing on any single token page is trapped on that page; they can't discover the rest of their project. Mixing _blank everywhere also spawns orphan tabs with no back-affordance, which is disorienting on mobile.
- **Recommendation:** Introduce one shared lightweight branded header component (logo + studio name, optional 'Your project' link to the portal when a portal token is known) used by g/shop/pay/book/d. Standardize link targets (in-journey links stay same-tab; only external links use _blank).

### Broken module handoffs around a contact

**`crm-clients-2` — Marking a deal 'Won' does not offer to promote it to a Client**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · ux · _CRM — Clients & Contacts_  
- **Evidence:** WonModal (leads/[id]/page.js:220-247) only captures sale amount; handleWonConfirm (919-926) sets status + actual_sale and stops. The is_client flag is only set by the separate 'Move to Clients' button (1458) or moveToClient (929). Backend status route (server.js:1911-1934) never touches is_client.
- **Problem:** The two halves of 'this person is now a customer' are decoupled. Studios will mark deals Won (for analytics) but forget the second manual step, so the Clients page silently under-represents the real client base and the feature looks empty/broken.
- **Recommendation:** Add a checkbox 'Also move to Clients' (default checked) inside WonModal, and in handleWonConfirm call leadsAPI.setClient(leadId, true) when checked. Keep the standalone button for leads won earlier. One-line UX change that makes the whole Clients module populate naturally.

**`contracts-3` — No notification or handoff when a client VIEWS or DECLINES a document**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · integration · standard: Global Notifications · _Contracts Studio_  
- **Evidence:** Global notify() is called only on sign (contracts-studio.js:979). The 'viewed' transition (backend:931-934) and the decline route (backend:985-994) emit only an SSE cs_updated broadcast and a cs_events row — no notifications insert, no lead timeline entry on view, no owner alert on decline.
- **Problem:** A client opening a proposal is a high-value sales signal ("strike while hot"), and a DECLINE is a critical churn signal. Today the owner gets neither unless they happen to have the Contracts tab open for the live SSE. A declined deal silently sits as 'declined' in a list the user may not revisit.
- **Recommendation:** Call notify() (the same helper used at :979) on first view ('Client viewed "{title}"', url to /leads/{id}) and on decline ('Client declined "{title}"', include reason). Also add a contact_history row on view/decline via addContactHistory so it lands on the lead timeline. All infrastructure already exists; this is a few lines.

**`invoices-store-6` — Print Store is undiscoverable — not in the primary NavBar**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · discoverability · _Invoices, Payments & Store_  
- **Evidence:** NavBar.js item list includes Invoices at 172 but no Store/Print Store entry (grep for store/shop/print in NavBar.js returns nothing). /studio/store is reachable only via a 'Print store' ghost button inside studio/page.js:184.
- **Problem:** An entire revenue module (sell prints/albums/digitals) is hidden behind a secondary button inside Media Studio. First-time users will not find it, undermining the 'one cohesive OS' goal where money features should be peers of Invoices.
- **Recommendation:** Add a NavBar entry (e.g. 'Store' or group Invoices+Store under a 'Sales/Billing' section) pointing to /studio/store, respecting any plan flag. Cheap, high cohesion payoff.

**`integration-1` — Winning a lead doesn't create a Client — Clients module silently stays empty**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · integration · _integration_  
- **Evidence:** server.js:1911-1934 (status='Closed - Won' updates only the lead + contact_history, never is_client); is_client is set only by the separate PUT /api/leads/:id/client (server.js:1892-1909); the NBA engine returns null for Won leads (leads-list/page.js:50); the top-level Clients nav item (NavBar.js:170) loads only is_client=1 rows (clients/page.js:41).
- **Problem:** A photographer marks a deal 'Closed - Won' and expects it to show up under the prominent 'Clients' tab — but it doesn't, because is_client is a second, unprompted manual flag. The Clients module appears broken/empty to anyone who hasn't discovered the 'Move to Clients' action, and won revenue lives only in the Leads pipeline. This is the most-felt break in the 'one cohesive product' promise.
- **Recommendation:** In the /api/leads/:id/status handler, when status becomes 'Closed - Won', also set is_client=1 and client_since=COALESCE(client_since, CURRENT_TIMESTAMP) (additive, backward-compatible). Alternatively, surface a one-click 'Move to Clients' confirmation in the win flow. Keep the manual toggle for edge cases.

**`crm-clients-1` — Client profile omits Contracts and Bookings — the customer-360 is incomplete**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · integration · _CRM — Clients & Contacts_  
- **Evidence:** server.js:1794-1828 (GET /api/leads/:id returns notes, reminders, history, invoices, emailWorkflows — never contracts or bookings). leads/[id]/page.js:1216-1220 tab list = notes/reminders/history/invoices/emails only; LeadStudioSection (548) adds shoots client-side; nothing renders contracts or bookings. Yet contracts carry lead_id (contracts/page.js) and bookings carry lead_id (bookings/page.js:129).
- **Problem:** The lead/client detail page is the only 'single place to see everything about a client', but two of the most important client artifacts — signed contracts and scheduled shoots/bookings — are invisible there. A studio owner cannot answer 'has this client signed? is their shoot booked?' without leaving to two separate modules and searching by name.
- **Recommendation:** Extend GET /api/leads/:id to also return contracts (csAPI source table WHERE lead_id) and bookings WHERE lead_id, and add 'Contracts' and 'Bookings' tabs (with counts) to the existing tab array in leads/[id]/page.js:1216, mirroring the Invoices tab pattern. Reuse existing status pills from contracts/page.js STATUS map for consistency. Additive, no schema change.

**`studio-library-5` — Project page shows the linked CRM client as dead text — no navigation back to the lead, and no booking/contract/invoice handoff**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · integration · _Media Studio — Library, Cull & AI_  
- **Evidence:** studio/[id]/page.js:355 renders `project.client_name` inside ms-hero-kicker as plain text. project.lead_id is available (set at creation, NewProjectModal). emitToLead (media-studio.js:345) pushes media events INTO the lead timeline, but there is no reciprocal link OUT: grep of [id]/page.js for `/leads/`, `/dashboard`, `lead_id` navigation = none. Milestones API exists (mediaAPI.milestones) but is never surfaced on the project page either.
- **Problem:** The shoot is an island. A photographer mid-delivery cannot jump to the client's CRM record to check the contract, balance due, or booking — they must context-switch via the app-switcher and search by name. For a product whose thesis is 'one operating system', the flagship media object has no outbound link to the customer record it belongs to.
- **Recommendation:** Make the client name a link that opens the lead (new tab, same-origin like the app-switcher does). Add a compact 'Client' card on the project page showing the linked lead with quick links to its contract / invoice / booking if present. Surface project milestones here too. Additive, reuses existing lead routes.

**`studio-video-7` — Finished reels have no handoff to Portfolio, Gallery, or the lead timeline**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · integration · standard: Integration / handoffs · _Media Studio — Video & Reel_  
- **Evidence:** ms_portfolio_items supports kind='video' and shapePortfolioItem handles video_url (media-studio.js:2363-2370), yet no endpoint or UI adds a rendered export to a portfolio. Galleries deliver photos; no path attaches an MP4. emitToLead exists for galleries (media-studio.js:345) but reel render/export calls it nowhere.
- **Problem:** The reel is a download-only cul-de-sac. The most valuable output of the module cannot flow into the creator's public portfolio, a client gallery/delivery, or the client's activity record — breaking the 'modules integrate into one product' goal exactly where it matters most.
- **Recommendation:** Add 'Add to portfolio' and 'Attach to gallery/delivery' actions on a completed export (write ms_portfolio_items / link to the gallery), and emit a lead activity ('Highlight reel delivered') so the render shows on the client timeline.

**`contracts-2` — Lead detail page has zero contract integration — no document list, no create CTA**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · integration · standard: Activity Timeline / cross-module hand-off · _Contracts Studio_  
- **Evidence:** leads/[id]/page.js (2969 lines) imports no csAPI and references no contract UI (grep for csAPI/contract/cs/ returns nothing relevant). The backend list endpoint already supports ?lead_id= filtering (contracts-studio.js:454), so the data path exists — only the panel is missing. Contracts reach the lead only as free-text contact_history rows (backend:916,966).
- **Problem:** From a lead — the natural hub of a relationship — you cannot see which contracts/proposals exist for that client, their status, or create a new one. The user must leave the lead, open the separate Contracts tab, and re-find the client in the Vault. This breaks the "every document lives inside the relationship" promise the help page itself makes (help/page.js:14).
- **Recommendation:** Add a "Documents" section/tab to the lead detail page that calls csAPI.list({ lead_id }) and renders status pills + a "New document" button pre-linked to this lead (router.push to NewDocModal with lead prefilled). Reuse the existing Pill component and row styling from page.js for consistency.

**`integration-2` — Lead profile is a dead-end: no 'New Contract / New Shoot / Share Portal' actions**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · integration · _integration_  
- **Evidence:** leads/[id]/page.js:1302-1328 quick actions are only Invoice, Email, Schedule, WA-Call. csAPI.create accepts lead_id (contracts/page.js:191) and mediaAPI.createProject accepts lead_id (studio/page.js:35-38, backend media-studio.js:408) and clientPortalAPI.link exists (api.js:508) — but none are reachable from the lead. LeadStudioSection returns null when shoots.length===0 (leads/[id]/page.js:555), hiding even the 'New shoot' button, which itself just opens /studio with no lead context (line 580).
- **Problem:** The lead profile is the natural command center for a client relationship, yet the user cannot start the two highest-value workflows (a contract or a shoot) from it, nor share the unified portal. They must switch to a different module's shell and re-find the same client by name — re-entering context the system already has.
- **Recommendation:** Add lead-profile quick actions: 'New contract' -> /contracts?new=1&lead_id=, 'New shoot' -> /studio?new=1&lead_id=, and 'Share client portal' (calls clientPortalAPI.link, copies /client/[token]). Always render the Studio section with a 'Start a shoot' button even at zero shoots, and pass lead_id through the deep links so modals prefill the client.

**`galleries-6` — Client collections and proofing selections don't flow into album building**  
MED · M effort · high impact · integration · standard: Actionable handoffs · _Galleries, Albums & Portfolio (client delivery)_  
- **Evidence:** Client 'Save selection as a collection' writes ms_fav_collections (media-studio.js:2655) and proofing submit closes a set (:2754), both surfaced to the photographer only as counts/notifications. The backend already has POST /api/media/galleries/:id/album-from-favorites (:744) but it is not wired to the collection/proofing UI, and neither album editor offers 'build from client's picks'.
- **Problem:** The whole point of proofing/favorites is to act on them. Today the photographer sees 'Client saved a collection (40 photos)' but has no one-click path to turn those 40 into an album or a filtered export — they must manually re-select. This breaks the delivery → album → upsell loop.
- **Recommendation:** Add a 'Create album from selection' / 'Export these' action on submitted proofing sets and saved collections in the shoot page, calling the existing album-from-favorites endpoint and routing into the canonical album editor. Closes the highest-value handoff gap in the module.

**`booking-8` — No project/invoice/deposit handoff out of a booking**  
MED · L effort · high impact · integration · _Booking & Scheduling_  
- **Evidence:** backend/booking.js contains zero references to projects or invoices (grep for project|invoice returns nothing). Services carry a price (DEFAULTS.services price:0, booking.js:47) and the public page renders '· $X' (book/[slug]/page.js:63), but booking only creates a lead + reminder + history.
- **Problem:** A booked paid session is a natural trigger to (a) spin up a project and (b) raise a deposit invoice — both modules exist in the app. Today the studio re-keys the client into a project and manually invoices, defeating the 'pipeline that flows' goal. Pricing is collected and then thrown away.
- **Recommendation:** Add an optional, settings-gated handoff: per-service toggles 'create project on booking' and 'request deposit (% or fixed)'. On booking, optionally create a project linked to the lead and/or a draft invoice using the existing invoice module. Keep it additive and off by default so existing behavior is preserved.

**`crm-clients-7` — Client detail still presents as a Lead — pipeline stepper and Won/Lost buttons persist after conversion**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · consistency · _CRM — Clients & Contacts_  
- **Evidence:** leads/[id]/page.js:1443-1456 always renders the pipeline steps + Won/Lost buttons; only an extra 'Client' chip is added when is_client (1464-1469). Page chrome/labeling is 'Lead' throughout.
- **Problem:** Once someone is a client, asking them to be re-marked Won/Lost and showing the acquisition pipeline is conceptually wrong and clutters the view. The page never reframes itself for the post-sale relationship, so Clients feels like a flag bolted onto Leads rather than a first-class entity.
- **Recommendation:** When lead.is_client, collapse/hide the acquisition pipeline + Won/Lost controls (or move them behind a 'reopen' affordance), retitle the header context to 'Client', and prioritize post-sale actions (new invoice, send contract, book shoot) in the right rail.

**`integration-5` — Lead permanent-delete orphans contracts, bookings, shoots, meetings and portal rows**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · data-integrity · _integration_  
- **Evidence:** server.js:1953-1962 permanent delete removes only notes, reminders, messages, contact_history, invoices, then the lead — leaving cs_documents, bookings, ms_projects (and ms_assets), meetings, activity_timeline, client_portals, ms_print_orders, lead_channels, lead_tags with a dangling lead_id. Contrast the merge handler (server.js:2012-2023) which dynamically discovers every lead_id table and reassigns it.
- **Problem:** After purging a lead, contracts/bookings/projects still reference the deleted id, the client portal token still resolves to a now-missing lead (server.js:5502 returns 404 mid-aggregation), and analytics/orphan rows linger. The merge path already proves the right pattern exists in the codebase but delete ignores it.
- **Recommendation:** Reuse the merge handler's dynamic lead_id-table discovery (server.js:2013-2015) in the permanent-delete path to cascade-delete (or null-out) every child row, including client_portals and activity_timeline. Additive, no schema change.

**`settings-team-10` — Knowledge memories have no outbound link to where the AI uses them - broken value loop**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · integration · _Settings, Team, Onboarding & Knowledge_  
- **Evidence:** knowledge/page.js subtitle 'Teach your AI about your business' (line 279) and source badges (540-541), but no link to the Inbox/AI-reply surface. handleLearnFromMessages (162) pulls FROM chats; there is no 'see this in action' link OUT. settings AICommandTab (3004) configures the AI profile separately with no cross-link to Knowledge.
- **Problem:** Users build a knowledge base in isolation with no confirmation it changes AI behavior and no path to the surface that consumes it; Knowledge feels disconnected from the rest of the 'one OS' - exactly the cohesion problem this audit targets.
- **Recommendation:** Add a 'Used in AI replies' explainer + a button linking to the Inbox (or AI Command settings) from the Knowledge header, surface a 'grounded in N memories' indicator on AI-suggested replies, and cross-link AICommandTab and Knowledge so AI setup feels like one flow.

**`booking-6` — Booking activity is visually indistinguishable in the lead Timeline/History**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · consistency · _Booking & Scheduling_  
- **Evidence:** Create/reschedule/cancel write contact_history with type 'booking' (booking.js:165,192,205), but HISTORY_ICONS and HISTORY_COLORS in leads/[id]/page.js:1228-1229 have no 'booking' key, so the renderer falls back to the generic Activity icon and grey #6b7280 (page.js:2146-2147).
- **Problem:** Inside the lead — the one place the two halves of the product meet — a booking looks identical to a status change. The user can't scan the timeline and spot 'this lead booked a session'. Undercuts the integration that the backend actually did the work to create.
- **Recommendation:** Add `booking: Calendar` to HISTORY_ICONS and a distinct color (e.g. the accent indigo #6366f1 used on the public page) to HISTORY_COLORS. One-line change, high cohesion payoff.

**`integration-6` — Invoices page has no create action and invoices can't link back to their source contract/booking**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · integration · _integration_  
- **Evidence:** invoices/page.js header has only a Back button (lines 423-431); the empty state says 'Create invoices from any lead profile' (line 491) — there is no New Invoice button anywhere on the Invoices screen. The invoices table (server.js:331-354) has lead_id but no contract_id/booking_id; auto-created invoices record origin only as free-text notes (contracts-studio.js:281).
- **Problem:** A user who opens Invoices to bill a walk-in (no lead) is stuck — the only creation path is buried on a lead profile. And an invoice auto-created from a signed contract can't be traced back to that contract except by reading a note string, so the billing<->agreement relationship is invisible in the UI.
- **Recommendation:** Add a 'New invoice' button on the Invoices page (reuse InvoiceModal from leads/[id]/page.js:288 with an optional client picker that find-or-creates a lead). Add nullable source_type/source_id columns to invoices, populate them in runAutomations, and render a 'From: <contract>' link on the invoice detail.

**`integration-7` — Public bookings never create a shoot or a calendar event**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · integration · _integration_  
- **Evidence:** booking.js:134-171 creates/finds a lead, inserts a bookings row, a reminder, contact_history, and a WhatsApp confirmation — but never inserts ms_projects (no shoot) and never inserts meetings / a Google Calendar event (the calendar insert lives only in the manual flow at server.js:5384).
- **Problem:** A client books a wedding date but the photographer gets a bare calendar-less booking row and no project to ingest photos into — they must manually create the shoot and (if they use Google Calendar) re-enter the appointment. The booking module is well-wired to the lead but stops short of the production and calendar systems.
- **Recommendation:** On booking confirmation, optionally (a) create an ms_projects shoot (lead_id, project_type from service, shoot_date from start_at) and (b) when workspace_integrations has a Google token, mirror the booking into a meetings calendar event. Gate both behind booking settings so existing behavior is unchanged.

**`galleries-11` — Portfolio share doesn't log CRM activity the way gallery publish does**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · integration · standard: Activity Timeline consistency · _Galleries, Albums & Portfolio (client delivery)_  
- **Evidence:** Gallery publish writes audit + emitToLead + (for collections/comments) addContactHistory and notify (media-studio.js:1656-1670, 2669-2670). The portfolio share endpoint POST /api/media/portfolio/share (:2555) sends over WhatsApp but, unlike publish, does not call addContactHistory/emitToLead, so 'sent portfolio to <client>' never appears on the lead's timeline.
- **Problem:** Breaks the otherwise-consistent rule that client-facing sends are recorded on the lead. A photographer reviewing a lead can't see that a portfolio was sent, creating a blind spot in the unified activity timeline.
- **Recommendation:** Mirror the publish path: in sharePortfolio, on successful WhatsApp send, call addContactHistory(lead_id, ...,'media','Sent portfolio') and emit the activity, so portfolio sends show up alongside gallery sends.

### Unbranded, fragmented public client journey

**`client-portal-2` — Gallery — the most-shared client link — shows no studio name and is footer-branded 'WappFlow'**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · consistency · standard: Workspace Branding · _Client Portal & the public client journey (client/[token], g/[token], d/[token], shop/[token], pay/[token], book/[slug], booking/manage/[token], folio/[handle])_  
- **Evidence:** g/[token]/page.js:180 hardcodes the eyebrow 'Your Gallery'; the header only shows the gallery title (line 181); the footer (line 269) reads 'Delivered with WappFlow Media Studio'. The backend response (media-studio.js:2626-2632) returns title/assets but NO brand. Contrast with portal/shop/book which all show company_name.
- **Problem:** Photo delivery is the link most often forwarded to family/guests. It presents as a WappFlow page, not the photographer's. This is the single biggest white-label leak in the client journey and is inconsistent with every other surface.
- **Recommendation:** Return brand (company_name) + company_logo from /api/media/portal/:token, render them in the gallery header, and change the footer to '{brand}' (optionally 'Delivered with WappFlow' only on free plan). Reuse the brandSym() helper that print-store.js:40 already implements.

**`client-portal-5` — Unpaid invoices in the portal are not clickable to pay**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · ux · standard: Workflow completeness · _Client Portal & the public client journey (client/[token], g/[token], d/[token], shop/[token], pay/[token], book/[slug], booking/manage/[token], folio/[handle])_  
- **Evidence:** client/[token]/page.js:85-87 renders each invoice as a non-link Row with an INV_STATUS pill ('Due'/'Overdue') and amount, but no href. The Row component only becomes a link when href is passed (page.js:50). The pay surface (/pay/[token]) exists and works.
- **Problem:** The portal shows a client they owe money and even flags it 'Overdue' in red, then gives them no way to pay — the highest-intent action on the page is a dead end, forcing the studio to chase a separate pay link.
- **Recommendation:** Have the portal backend join invoices to their payment token (or the existing /pay link) and pass href={`/pay/${payToken}`} on unpaid invoices so the row is tappable. Show a 'Pay now' affordance on the right for sent/overdue invoices.

**`client-portal-8` — Portal link is buried in Contracts Vault and absent from the CRM lead/client detail**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · discoverability · standard: Feature Discoverability · _Client Portal & the public client journey (client/[token], g/[token], d/[token], shop/[token], pay/[token], book/[slug], booking/manage/[token], folio/[handle])_  
- **Evidence:** clientPortalAPI.link is referenced in exactly one component — contracts/vault/page.js:23 — and the 'Copy client portal link' button only appears inside a collapsed per-client accordion (vault/page.js:69). Grep for 'portal'/'/client/' in app/page.js (main CRM), app/clients, and app/leads returned zero matches.
- **Problem:** The flagship 'one link for the whole client relationship' is undiscoverable from where studios actually work the client (the lead/client record). Most users will never find it, so the unified portal effectively doesn't exist for them.
- **Recommendation:** Surface a 'Copy/Share client portal link' action on the lead/client detail screen (and ideally a Share menu alongside gallery/contract share). Reuse clientPortalAPI.link; it already lazily creates the token on first call (server.js:5493).

**`integration-3` — Unified Client Portal — the best cohesion feature — is buried and never auto-shared**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · discoverability · _integration_  
- **Evidence:** The aggregating portal endpoint server.js:5498-5526 returns galleries+documents+invoices+albums+orders+milestones+projects for a lead. The link is generated/copied in exactly one place: contracts/vault/page.js:23. Gallery publish sends only the bare /g/[token] (media-studio.js:1651); contract send sends /d/[token]; nothing sends /client/[token].
- **Problem:** WappFlow already built the single most 'operating-system-like' surface — one client-facing hub for everything — but it's hidden inside Contracts Vault and never offered at the moments the client actually receives a deliverable. Clients get a scatter of disconnected links instead of one home, and most studios will never know the hub exists.
- **Recommendation:** Surface the /client/[token] link on the lead profile, the Clients card (clients/page.js), and the gallery-publish / contract-send dialogs. Offer 'Send portal link' as the default share so the client lands on the full hub; keep the per-asset link as a secondary option.

**`galleries-4` — Client delivery gallery carries zero studio branding**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · integration · standard: Workspace Branding · _Galleries, Albums & Portfolio (client delivery)_  
- **Evidence:** g/[token]/page.js hardcodes the accent gold (#c2a878 throughout, e.g. :144,:180,:198) and a generic footer 'Delivered with WappFlow Media Studio' (:269). grep for logo/brand/studio_name/avatar/customer_name in that file returns nothing. The portal API (media-studio.js:2626) returns only title/download_policy/watermark/assets — no studio identity. Meanwhile the portfolio fully supports branding (accent, avatar, studio name, 10 themes via shapePortfolio/PortfolioCanvas).
- **Problem:** The gallery is the photographer's most client-facing artifact, yet it looks like WappFlow, not like the studio. Branding maturity already exists for the portfolio; galleries should inherit it. This undercuts perceived professionalism and the 'workspace branding' product standard.
- **Recommendation:** Reuse the portfolio's branding data (studio title, avatar/logo, accent) on the gallery: have the portal GET return the workspace's studio identity, and parameterize g/[token]'s accent color + header logo + footer from it. This is additive (fallback to current gold), reuses an existing data source, and unifies the two client-facing surfaces.

**`client-portal-1` — Studio logo is never shown to clients on any public surface**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · integration · standard: Workspace Branding · _Client Portal & the public client journey (client/[token], g/[token], d/[token], shop/[token], pay/[token], book/[slug], booking/manage/[token], folio/[handle])_  
- **Evidence:** company_settings.company_logo column exists (server.js:302) and is uploaded via /api/.../logo (server.js:1386-1389), but the portal (client/[token]/page.js:57), shop (shop/[token]/page.js:52), book (book/[slug]/page.js:53), pay (pay/[token]/page.js has no header at all), and booking-manage (booking/manage/[token]/page.js:38) all render a hardcoded first-letter avatar on a fixed linear-gradient(135deg,#0ea5e9,#6366f1). The gallery backend (media-studio.js:2626-2632) doesn't even return brand name.
- **Problem:** The client never sees the studio's actual brand mark. Every page that the studio sends to paying clients is visibly a generic template, which undercuts the 'one branded product' goal and makes a premium studio look low-rent.
- **Recommendation:** Add company_logo (and an optional brand/accent color) to the public payload of all surfaces (already trivial for shop/book/pay/portal which already read company_settings; add brand+logo to the gallery portal response in media-studio.js:2626). In each header, render the logo image when present and fall back to the letter avatar. Drive the accent color from one brand color instead of hardcoded hexes.

**`client-portal-3` — Public links have no per-studio metadata; every shared link previews as generic WappFlow marketing copy**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · discoverability · standard: Link-preview/SEO metadata · _Client Portal & the public client journey (client/[token], g/[token], d/[token], shop/[token], pay/[token], book/[slug], booking/manage/[token], folio/[handle])_  
- **Evidence:** All public routes are 'use client' with no generateMetadata (verified: client/g/d/shop/pay/book/folio all start with 'use client' and none export metadata). Titles are only set client-side via document.title after fetch (e.g. portal/page.js:29, shop/page.js:18). The root layout.js:13-24 sets OG title 'WappFlow — AI-powered customer operations'.
- **Problem:** When a studio shares a gallery/portal/contract link on WhatsApp/iMessage/Instagram, the rich preview shows WappFlow's CRM marketing copy and no studio name or cover image — actively confusing the client and leaking the platform brand on the studio's most-shared assets.
- **Recommendation:** Split each public route into a server page.js (export async generateMetadata fetching brand/title/cover for OG title+image+description) wrapping the existing client component. Per AGENTS.md, read the Next 16 docs for the current generateMetadata + dynamic-route signature before implementing.

**`client-portal-4` — The 'unified' portal omits booking, pay, and shop — it is not actually unified**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · integration · standard: Cross-module integration · _Client Portal & the public client journey (client/[token], g/[token], d/[token], shop/[token], pay/[token], book/[slug], booking/manage/[token], folio/[handle])_  
- **Evidence:** server.js comment at line 5483 calls it 'one branded link per client across all modules', but the response (server.js:5518-5524) returns only galleries, documents, invoices, albums, orders, milestones, projects. No booking link, no pay link, no shop. The portal page (client/[token]/page.js) has no Section for any of them.
- **Problem:** The one screen designed to be the client's home base can't route them to half the things the studio offers, so the studio still has to send 4-5 separate links per client — the exact fragmentation the portal was meant to solve.
- **Recommendation:** Extend the portal payload to include the workspace booking slug (→ /book), any open pay links for this lead (→ /pay), and the shop link when a published gallery with store_enabled exists. Add 'Book a session', 'Payments due', and 'Order prints' sections to client/[token]/page.js, reusing the existing Row/Section components.

**`client-portal-6` — Conversion success states are dead ends with no next step**  
MED · M effort · high impact · ux · standard: Workflow completeness / handoffs · _Client Portal & the public client journey (client/[token], g/[token], d/[token], shop/[token], pay/[token], book/[slug], booking/manage/[token], folio/[handle])_  
- **Evidence:** After signing, d/[token]/page.js:95-100 shows a 'Signed — thank you' banner (optionally a PDF link) and stops. shop/[token]/page.js:40-46 'Order placed!' is terminal. book/[slug]/page.js:40-47 'You're booked!' is terminal. pay/[token]/page.js:23-28 'Payment received' is terminal. None link back to the client portal or the logical next action.
- **Problem:** Every successful client action drops them off a cliff. A client who just signed a proposal has no nudge to pay the deposit or book the shoot; a client who just booked can't get to their galleries/invoices. The journey feels like disconnected transactions, not one relationship.
- **Recommendation:** When the lead has a client_portals token, append a 'View everything for your project →' button linking to /client/{token} on each success state, plus a contextual next-step CTA (signed→pay deposit, booked→portal, paid→portal). Pass the portal token through the public payloads.

**`client-portal-9` — Gallery favourite/comment/download actions are hover-only on the grid**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · ux · standard: Accessibility / Responsive · _Client Portal & the public client journey (client/[token], g/[token], d/[token], shop/[token], pay/[token], book/[slug], booking/manage/[token], folio/[handle])_  
- **Evidence:** g/[token]/page.js:247-248 reveals the figcaption (heart/comment/download) via onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave opacity toggling. On touch devices these are unreachable in the grid; the comment at line 286 acknowledges the lightbox is the touch fallback, meaning a client must open each photo full-screen to favourite it.
- **Problem:** On phones — where most clients view galleries — favouriting requires opening every photo into the lightbox first, turning a quick 'tap the ones I love' into a slow one-by-one chore. Hover-only also fails keyboard/screen-reader users.
- **Recommendation:** Always show a small persistent heart affordance on each grid tile (or reveal on tap/focus, not just hover). The lightbox controls can remain as the rich path; the grid just needs a tap-target so favouriting works without opening each image.

**`client-portal-10` — Inconsistent failure/expiry states and identity capture across surfaces**  
LOW · M effort · medium impact · consistency · standard: Error/empty-state + terminology consistency · _Client Portal & the public client journey (client/[token], g/[token], d/[token], shop/[token], pay/[token], book/[slug], booking/manage/[token], folio/[handle])_
