- **Problem:** A studio with /bookings open sees no new bookings arrive — they must reload. The infrastructure to make it real-time already exists and is being paid for (the broadcast fires), but the consumer is missing.
- **Recommendation:** Subscribe to the existing workspace SSE stream on the bookings page and, on data.type==='booking_created', re-fetch bookingAPI.list() (or append). This matches the documented es.onmessage switch pattern and is a few lines.

**`comms-11` — Typed message is lost when switching channels (no draft persistence)**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · ux · _Communications (Team Chat + Project Rooms + Huddles/Calls + Floating WhatsApp Chat)_  
- **Evidence:** handleChannelSelect resets messages/replyTo/editing and loads the new channel (page.js:518-526) but the contentEditable input (inputRef) is never saved; its innerHTML is only cleared on successful send (handleSend line 557). Switching channels mid-compose silently discards the draft.
- **Problem:** Standard chat clients keep a per-channel draft. Losing a half-written message on an accidental channel click is a real, felt friction in daily use.
- **Recommendation:** Keep a drafts map keyed by channel id (in state or localStorage); on channel select, stash the current input HTML and restore the target channel's draft. Small, additive, big perceived-quality win.

**`studio-library-6` — Studio Brain recommendation is recomputed (with table writes) on every cull-page load**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · performance · _Media Studio — Library, Cull & AI_  
- **Evidence:** cull/page.js:99 calls brainsAPI.recommendations() on mount. That endpoint (brains.js:123-137) calls deriveCreatorBrain() AND deriveStyle() unconditionally; deriveCreatorBrain runs INSERT...ON CONFLICT for several keys (brains.js:56-58) and deriveStyle scans up to 5000 score rows then UPSERTs a style_profiles row (brains.js:69-90) — on a GET, every page open.
- **Problem:** A read-path GET performs aggregate scans + multiple writes each time any user opens a cull screen. On a busy workspace this is needless write amplification and lock contention on SQLite, and couples a cheap UI hint to an expensive recompute. It is also a subtle data-integrity smell (GET mutates).
- **Recommendation:** Cache the derived brain/style (updated_at already exists) and recompute lazily — e.g. only re-derive if stale (>N hours) or when cull decisions changed since last derive. Have GET /recommendations read the cached style_profiles/creator_brain rows and only call derive on an explicit /derive POST (which already exists). Small change in brains.js.

**`crm-leads-10` — Reminders are a passive list with no due-time notification, and the 'upcoming' query has no time window**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · integration · _CRM — Leads & Pipeline_  
- **Evidence:** GET /api/reminders/upcoming comment says 'due within 48h' but the query has no date predicate — it returns all incomplete reminders for the user, LIMIT 50 (server.js:2097-2108). Reminders render in the per-lead tab and the SidePanel Calendar (SidePanel.js:35-63) but nothing fires a notification when one comes due; notify()/sendPush exist for new leads (server.js:1860-1861) but are not wired to reminders.
- **Problem:** Follow-up is the named high-priority area, yet a set reminder will silently pass its time — the user must remember to open the panel. This makes the whole reminder feature low-trust and undermines the follow-up workflow.
- **Recommendation:** Add a date window to the upcoming query and a lightweight scheduled check (or on-login sweep) that calls notify()/sendPushToUser for reminders whose reminder_date has passed and is uncompleted, deep-linking to /leads/${lead_id}. Surface due reminders in the NavBar bell feed.

**`studio-library-7` — Project detail polls the full asset list (limit 5000) every 20s, re-setting state mid-interaction**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · performance · _Media Studio — Library, Cull & AI_  
- **Evidence:** studio/[id]/page.js:195 `poll = setInterval(() => { refreshAssets(); refreshGalleries(); }, 20000)`. refreshAssets (:179) does listAssets with limit:5000 and setAssets(...) wholesale. The grid renders up to visibleCount(400) items; a wholesale setAssets re-renders the masonry every 20s regardless of whether the user is selecting or scrolling.
- **Problem:** On large shoots this is a heavy refetch + full re-render on a fixed timer, purely to pick up client favourites/comments. It can cause visible jank and, combined with selection state, risks surprising the user during multi-select. The galleries refresh is the only part that truly needs polling (favourites/comments live on galleries, not assets).
- **Recommendation:** Poll only refreshGalleries() on the interval (favourites/comments live there); refresh assets only after an action (upload/watermark/edit) or via the existing broadcastToWorkspace SSE events (ms_gallery_published etc. already broadcast). If asset polling is kept, diff by id/variants before setAssets to avoid re-rendering unchanged items.

**`comms-7` — Voice notes upload but render as a file-download link, not a player**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · ux · _Communications (Team Chat + Project Rooms + Huddles/Calls + Floating WhatsApp Chat)_  
- **Evidence:** startRecording uploads a webm blob through chatAPI.sendMedia (page.js:689-708). The backend sets media_type to 'image' or 'file' only (server.js:2800). MessageBubble renders non-image media as a FileText download link (page.js:243-252) with no <audio> element. There is no waveform/duration/inline-play.
- **Problem:** A 'voice note' that you must download to hear is not a voice note. The feature is advertised (mic button + 'Recording… tap to send') but the playback experience is a generic attachment, undermining the feature.
- **Recommendation:** Detect audio mime (or originalname endsWith .webm/.m4a) on the server to set media_type='audio', and in MessageBubble render an inline <audio controls src=media_url> (optionally a compact play+duration pill). Small additive change reusing the existing media pipeline.

**`comms-8` — RoomPanel polls every 6s despite SSE being available**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · performance · _Communications (Team Chat + Project Rooms + Huddles/Calls + Floating WhatsApp Chat)_  
- **Evidence:** RoomPanel sets setInterval(() => load(cid), 6000) (RoomPanel.js:46) and re-fetches the full last-50 messages each tick. The main chat already replaced polling with SSE (page.js:418-467, comment line 416 'replaces the old 3s poll'). afterMessage already broadcasts chat_message to the whole workspace, so room messages are on the bus.
- **Problem:** Every open lead/project/contract detail page issues a getMessages call every 6 seconds per viewer — N tabs × M open entities of constant DB load, and a 6s latency on a feature meant to feel live. It also duplicates the transport the rest of comms abandoned.
- **Recommendation:** Subscribe RoomPanel to the shared SSE stream and filter chat_message frames by channel_id === room channel; drop the interval (or keep a slow 60s reconcile as a safety net). Consider a tiny shared SSE context so chat + RoomPanel + NavBar share one EventSource instead of each opening its own.

**`platform-11` — Notification feed misses Studio, Media jobs, and Comms unread — partial cross-module reach**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · integration · standard: Global Notifications / Activity Timeline · _Platform Plumbing (entitlements, pricing, plan UX, notifications, confirm, AI copilots, trash, WhatsApp, API client)_  
- **Evidence:** NavBar.js:308-318 builds the feed from notificationsAPI.get (described as contracts/bookings/gallery/cross-platform leads), today's leads, and reminders. No call to commsAPI.unread (api.js:282) or mediaAPI.jobs (api.js:363) / video export completion. lib/unread.js (per-lead unread) is consumed only by leads-list/dashboard, not surfaced in the bell.
- **Problem:** The global bell does not reflect unread team chat (Comms), finished/failed Media render jobs, or per-lead unread — so the 'one place to see what needs attention' is incomplete and users must visit each module to find out. Undercuts the single-OS goal.
- **Recommendation:** Fold commsAPI.unread count and mediaAPI.jobs (failed/completed) into loadNotifData (or the proposed summary endpoint) and render them as feed items with deep links. Surface lib/unread badge count in the bell too.

**`frontend-perf-6` — FloatingChat (global) and each page both open their own EventSource to /api/events, doubling concurrent SSE connections**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** components/FloatingChat.js:65 opens `new EventSource(.../api/events?token=...)`, and FloatingChat is mounted globally inside NavBar (NavBar.js:713 `<FloatingChat />`). Simultaneously each major page opens its OWN second EventSource to the same endpoint: app/dashboard/page.js:665, app/chat/page.js:422, app/leads/[id]/page.js (grep confirms `new EventSource`), app/control/events/page.js. So on the dashboard the browser holds 2 long-lived connections to /api/events; on a lead profile, another 2. Each handler independently parses every frame.
- **Problem:** HTTP/1.1 browsers cap ~6 connections per origin and SSE holds each open indefinitely; running 2 redundant streams per page burns half that budget against the app's own API host and doubles server-side connection load and client-side JSON parsing of identical frames. It also risks starving other XHRs to the same host.
- **Recommendation:** Centralize a single shared EventSource in one provider (e.g. an SSEProvider in providers.js or a small lib/events.js singleton) and have FloatingChat, dashboard, chat, and leads/[id] subscribe to a shared emitter via context/callbacks instead of each constructing its own EventSource. This collapses N connections per page to 1 and de-duplicates frame parsing.

### God-pages, unbounded fetches & client-side data handling

**`frontend-perf-2` — Dashboard fans out up to 30 sequential getById calls for reminders an existing single endpoint already returns**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** app/dashboard/page.js:754-757: `const reminderPromises = leadsData.slice(0,30).map(lead => leadsAPI.getById(lead.id).then(r => r.data.reminders || []))` then `await Promise.all(reminderPromises)`. This issues up to 30 full lead-detail requests on every dashboard load just to collect reminders. A dedicated endpoint already exists: backend server.js:2097 `app.get('/api/reminders/upcoming', ...)` and is wired in lib/api.js:175 as `remindersAPI.getUpcoming()` — but the dashboard does not use it. NavBar.js:297 already uses getUpcoming() correctly, proving the endpoint works.
- **Problem:** The dashboard makes ~31 HTTP round-trips (1 leads + 30 lead-detail) where 2 would suffice, and silently shows reminders for only the first 30 leads (data-correctness bug: a reminder on the 31st-newest lead never appears). This is the slowest part of first-paint after login.
- **Recommendation:** Replace the slice(0,30).map(getById) block with a single `remindersAPI.getUpcoming()` call inside the existing `Promise.all` at dashboard/page.js:743. This removes ~30 requests, fixes the 30-lead truncation, and reuses code NavBar already relies on.

**`frontend-perf-3` — PlanProvider context value is rebuilt every render, re-rendering every usePlan() consumer app-wide**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** lib/plan.js:72-97 constructs a fresh `value` object literal (with freshly-created hasFeature/hasLimit/quotaFor/usageLevel/atLimit closures) on EVERY render of PlanProvider, then passes it to `<PlanContext.Provider value={value}>` (line 99). Nothing is wrapped in useMemo/useCallback. PlanProvider wraps the entire authenticated app (providers.js:13 → {children}) and its own `setInterval(fetchPlan, 5*60*1000)` (plan.js:43) calls setPlanInfo, forcing a provider re-render every 5 minutes. usePlan() is consumed in 9 files including the global NavBar (3x), UsageWarnings, PlanWelcomeModal, settings (4x), reports, leads-list, team, knowledge (grep: 15 call sites).
- **Problem:** Because the context value identity changes on every provider render, React notifies and re-renders every usePlan() subscriber even when plan data is unchanged — including large pages like settings (3173 lines) and leads-list (1325 lines). The 5-minute background refresh and any ancestor re-render cascades into the whole consumer tree.
- **Recommendation:** Wrap the `value` object in `useMemo(() => ({...}), [planInfo, loading, fetchPlan])` and wrap hasFeature/hasLimit/quotaFor/usageLevel/atLimit in useCallback (deps: [planInfo]). This is a few-line change that eliminates app-wide re-render churn with no behavior change.

**`frontend-perf-1` — Global NavBar refetches the ENTIRE leads table every 60s on every authenticated page**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** components/NavBar.js:277 sets `setInterval(loadNotifData, 60000)`. loadNotifData (NavBar.js:292-319) calls `leadsAPI.getAll(null)` (line 296) which hits `/api/leads` — backend server.js:1493-1532 returns the full leads table for the workspace with NO LIMIT/pagination (`SELECT * FROM leads ... ORDER BY last_message_at DESC`). The entire payload is fetched only to compute `today = all.filter(l => new Date(l.created_at).toDateString() === now.toDateString())` (NavBar.js:300) for a notification badge. NavBar is mounted on every authenticated route, so a studio with thousands of leads transfers + JSON-parses its whole CRM every minute, on every open tab, regardless of what page the user is on.
- **Problem:** A notification-badge count drives a full-table download every 60 seconds across the whole app. On a large workspace this is hundreds of KB of JSON parsed on the main thread every minute per tab, plus repeated DB scans server-side — pure waste that makes the app feel heavy and drains battery/bandwidth, with zero user-visible value beyond a single integer.
- **Recommendation:** Add a lightweight backend endpoint (e.g. GET /api/notifications/summary) that returns today-lead count + urgent-reminder count + unread feed as a small JSON object, and have loadNotifData call that instead of leadsAPI.getAll(null). Also raise the poll interval to ~120s or replace the poll entirely by consuming the SSE `lead_created` frames the app already emits (FloatingChat already holds an EventSource). Reuse the existing dedicated remindersAPI.getUpcoming() rather than deriving urgency client-side.

**`crm-leads-8` — Bulk 'Move to stage' and 'Move to Clients' fire one request per lead sequentially with no batched endpoint or progress UI**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · performance · _CRM — Leads & Pipeline_  
- **Evidence:** leads-list/page.js:970-979 loops `for (const id of ids) { await leadsAPI.updateStatus(id, {status}); }` and 996-1002 loops `await leadsAPI.setClient(id, true)` — both sequential awaits. A batched pattern already exists for trash: POST /api/leads/bulk-trash runs a single `UPDATE … WHERE id IN (…)` (server.js:5150-5163).
- **Problem:** Selecting 50 leads and moving them to a stage fires 50 serialized round-trips (seconds of frozen UI with no spinner), and individual failures are swallowed by empty catch blocks so the user gets a success toast even on partial failure. It's also inconsistent with bulk-trash/bulk-assign which are single calls.
- **Recommendation:** Add POST /api/leads/bulk-status and POST /api/leads/bulk-client mirroring bulk-trash (single IN-clause UPDATE in a transaction, addContactHistory per id, logAudit, SSE). Swap the frontend loops for one call each with a loading state on the bulk bar.

**`settings-team-4` — settings/page.js is a single ~3000-line file with 18 ungrouped, unsearchable tabs**  
HIGH · L effort · high impact · architecture · _Settings, Team, Onboarding & Knowledge_  
- **Evidence:** settings/page.js is 2946+ lines; TABS (lines 53-72) lists 18 flat tabs with no grouping; the render block (lines 2477-2494) is an 18-way activeTab=== chain; all tab components (CompanyTab, CurrencyTab, PresetsTab, EmailTemplatesTab, EmailSendingTab, EmailReceivingTab, AutoReplyTab, TagsTab, NotificationsTab, IntegrationsTab, WorkspaceTab, DataPrivacyTab, AICommandTab, ConnectionsTab, PasswordTab) are defined inline in the same file.
- **Problem:** The flat 18-tab list has no information architecture - related items (3 separate Email tabs) are not grouped and there is no search, so users cannot find a setting; the file is also unmaintainable and re-renders the whole tree.
- **Recommendation:** Without changing behavior: (a) group TABS into sections (Channels, Branding & Commerce, Communication, Team & Security, Account) with sidebar section headers; (b) add a tab-filter input atop the sidebar; (c) extract each *Tab into settings/tabs/*.js so the page is a thin shell. Keep the additive data-driven TABS array as the single source of truth.

**`frontend-perf-4` — Leads list renders every row unvirtualized and unmemoized, fed by an unpaginated all-leads fetch**  
HIGH · L effort · high impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** app/leads-list/page.js:1140 `leads.map((lead, i) => { ... })` renders ALL filtered leads directly into the DOM — each row is a 9-column CSS grid with inline style objects, two inline onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave handlers (lines 1148-1149), and multiple inline IIFEs (e.g. the platform-chip IIFE at 1165-1176 and the phone IIFE at 1183-1192). No react-window/virtuoso, no React.memo row component (grep for memo/LeadRow in the file returns nothing), no slice cap (unlike the dashboard which slices, and Studio which caps at visibleCount=400). The data comes from `leadsAPI.getAll(...)` (line 740) → backend server.js:1493 returns all rows unpaginated. `memberById` (line 829) does an O(n) `members.find` per row inside the map.
- **Problem:** A workspace with a few thousand leads mounts a few thousand complex grid rows at once, recreating all inline handlers/styles on every keystroke in the search box (applyFilters writes a new `leads` array → full remount). Typing in search, sorting, or tag-toggling visibly janks on large datasets — exactly the CRM screen power users live in.
- **Recommendation:** Extract the row into a `React.memo`'d <LeadRow> component (hoist the inline style/handler closures out of the map), and virtualize the list with react-window (or at minimum apply the same visibleCount-slice pattern Studio uses at studio/[id]/page.js:498). Longer-term, add server-side pagination/limit to /api/leads and load incrementally. Replace `members.find` per row with a memoized member map.

**`frontend-perf-5` — Dashboard eagerly bundles recharts + @hello-pangea/dnd; next/dynamic is used nowhere in the app**  
MED · M effort · high impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** app/dashboard/page.js statically imports `@hello-pangea/dnd` (line 5: DragDropContext/Droppable/Draggable) and `recharts` (lines 16-18: BarChart/Bar/PieChart/etc.), both heavy libraries, into the first authenticated screen. package.json confirms `@hello-pangea/dnd ^18.0.1` and `recharts ^3.8.1`. A repo-wide grep for `next/dynamic` returns ZERO matches in src/ — no component or library anywhere is code-split beyond Next's automatic per-route split. reports/page.js also statically pulls recharts.
- **Problem:** Recharts and a full drag-and-drop engine are shipped in the dashboard's initial JS even though the charts are below the fold and the Kanban board is one of several views. This inflates the post-login Time-to-Interactive on the most-visited authenticated page. The total absence of next/dynamic means no lazy boundaries exist anywhere to trim god-page bundles.
- **Recommendation:** Lazy-load the chart blocks and the DnD board via `next/dynamic(() => import(...), { ssr: false, loading: ... })` in dashboard/page.js (and the Recharts blocks in reports/page.js). This is additive and backward-compatible. Establishing one next/dynamic pattern also unlocks splitting other heavy widgets (AICommandCenter, RoomPanel/HuddleModal, the album/video editors).

**`contracts-11` — Document and lead lists are fetched unbounded (no pagination/LIMIT)**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · performance · standard: Performance / unbounded lists · _Contracts Studio_  
- **Evidence:** GET /cs/documents has no LIMIT and SELECTs d.* (loads the blocks column for every row even though shapeDoc omits it by default) — contracts-studio.js:455,216-224. NewDocModal and BulkSendModal call leadsAPI.getAll(null) to load ALL leads client-side (page.js:177, 276); the Overview itself loads all docs and filters in-memory (page.js:51-59).
- **Problem:** For a studio with thousands of documents/leads this loads the entire table on every Overview visit and every modal open, and the SQL pulls each document's full blocks JSON off disk into memory needlessly. The client-side filtering also means search/filter never hits an index. Fine today, a wall later — and it's a quiet one-OS-feel killer when the app gets sluggish at scale.
- **Recommendation:** Add pagination + a server-side search param to /cs/documents, and SELECT explicit columns (exclude blocks) for the list query. Make the New/Bulk modals query leads by search term server-side rather than getAll(null). Backend already filters by status/type/lead_id, so extending it is low-risk.

**`settings-team-7` — Every Settings tab refetches on mount with no cache; revisiting a tab re-hits the API**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · performance · _Settings, Team, Onboarding & Knowledge_  
- **Evidence:** PresetsTab fetchPresets (line 322), LostReasonsTab (417), EmailTemplatesTab (493), AutoReplyTab (604), TagsTab (716), NotificationsTab (807), IntegrationsContent (1040), DataPrivacyTab auditAPI.getLogs (1245), WorkspaceTab workspaceAPI.get (1409), ConnectionsTab (1770), EmailSendingTab (2524), EmailReceivingTab (2693), AICommandTab (3004). Tabs are conditionally rendered (lines 2477-2494) so each is unmounted when inactive and refetches on every revisit.
- **Problem:** Navigating away and back to any tab re-issues its network request with no stale-while-revalidate, making Settings feel slow on repeat visits and multiplying server load.
- **Recommendation:** Lift per-tab data into a lightweight cache, or keep tab components mounted with display:none, or adopt a simple SWR/react-query pattern so each tab fetches once per session. Low risk since each tab owns its own state.

**`settings-team-14` — Company/Currency tabs share mutable state with no unsaved-changes guard - edits lost on tab switch**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · ux · _Settings, Team, Onboarding & Knowledge_  
- **Evidence:** settings/page.js holds company state at page level (line 2359); CompanyTab and CurrencyTab both mutate it via setCompany and each has its own save button (lines 209, 300) calling handleSaveCompany. Switching tabs calls setActiveTab + window.scrollTo (line 2378) and re-renders, but nothing warns about pending edits; fetchCompany runs only once on mount.
- **Problem:** Editing Company profile or Currency then clicking another tab gives no 'unsaved changes' prompt; the user assumes it saved (list tabs auto-save per item) and loses work on the next reload - an inconsistency between auto-saving list tabs and manual-save form tabs.
- **Recommendation:** Track a dirty flag for the company form and either prompt on tab switch when dirty or standardize the save pattern. Cheaply: show a persistent 'Unsaved changes - Save' bar when company state differs from the last fetched value.

**`backend-perf-7` — Core list endpoints have no pagination/LIMIT (leads, invoices, contracts, lead messages)**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** server.js:1493-1536 GET /api/leads builds 'SELECT * FROM leads ... ORDER BY last_message_at DESC' with no LIMIT and then enriches every row in JS. server.js:2180-2185 GET /api/invoices 'SELECT * FROM invoices WHERE user_id=? ORDER BY created_at DESC' no LIMIT. contracts-studio.js:455 GET /api/cs/documents 'SELECT d.*, l.customer_name ... ORDER BY d.updated_at DESC' no LIMIT. server.js:1597-1613 GET /api/leads/:leadId/messages 'SELECT * FROM messages WHERE lead_id=? ORDER BY timestamp ASC' no LIMIT (and idx_messages_lead does not cover the timestamp sort).
- **Problem:** These return the entire table for the workspace/lead in one payload. A studio with thousands of leads or a chat thread with thousands of synced WhatsApp messages ships a multi-MB JSON and forces a full sort each request — the opposite of the snappy single-OS feel the product is targeting. The leads list also runs an extra full sort the index doesn't help with for the messages thread.
- **Recommendation:** Add limit/offset (or keyset) pagination to /api/leads, /api/invoices, /api/cs/documents consistent with the pattern already used at media-studio.js:848 and command-center.js:322. For lead messages, default to the most recent N (e.g. ORDER BY timestamp DESC LIMIT 100 then reverse, with a 'before' cursor like the chat handler at server.js:2755-2763) and add a composite index idx_messages_lead_ts ON messages(lead_id, timestamp) to cover the sort.

**`frontend-perf-7` — Studio project loads up to 5000 assets in a single fetch with no pagination**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** app/studio/[id]/page.js:180 `mediaAPI.listAssets(id, { limit: 5000 })` pulls up to 5000 asset records in one request on project open, stored in `assets` state. Rendering is mitigated (shown.slice(0, visibleCount) with visibleCount=400, studio/[id]/page.js:176/498, images use loading="lazy" at line 508), but the full 5000-row JSON is fetched, parsed, and held in memory, and `shown = ... assets.filter(...)` (line 175) recomputes on every render without useMemo.
- **Problem:** A large wedding/event shoot (multi-thousand assets) front-loads a multi-MB JSON parse and a large in-memory array before the user can interact, and re-filters the whole array on each render. The rendering layer is windowed but the data layer is not, so first-load latency scales with shoot size.
- **Recommendation:** Page the asset fetch (e.g. limit 400 + cursor) aligned with the existing visibleCount-based 'show more' UI so network and render grow together, and wrap the `shown` filter in useMemo([assets, mediaTab]). This keeps the proven lazy-render UX while removing the 5000-row up-front payload.

**`platform-8` — NavBar notification poll re-fetches ALL leads every 60s on every page just to compute a badge**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · performance · standard: Performance / Analytics · _Platform Plumbing (entitlements, pricing, plan UX, notifications, confirm, AI copilots, trash, WhatsApp, API client)_  
- **Evidence:** NavBar.js:277 `const poll = setInterval(loadNotifData, 60000)` and loadNotifData (NavBar.js:295) calls `leadsAPI.getAll(null)` (unbounded) purely to filter today's leads (line 300) and feed the badge. FloatingChat (FloatingChat.js:101) and AICommandCenter also call leadsAPI.getAll(null) — full lead list fetches.
- **Problem:** On a workspace with thousands of leads, every authenticated tab downloads the entire lead list once a minute (plus on every floating-chat open) just to derive a small "new today" count. This is wasteful bandwidth/CPU and contradicts the speed/polish goal.
- **Recommendation:** Add a lightweight `/api/notifications/summary` (or extend notificationsAPI.get) that returns counts + today's new-lead count server-side, and have NavBar consume that instead of getAll(null). Paginate/scope the floating-chat and command-center lead fetches.

**`crm-leads-15` — Leads list loads and filters the entire workspace client-side with no pagination or virtualization**  
MED · L effort · medium impact · performance · _CRM — Leads & Pipeline_  
- **Evidence:** fetchAll calls leadsAPI.getAll with only a client/platform/account filter (leads-list/page.js:734-744); the backend ORDER BY last_message_at DESC returns all rows (server.js:1493-1532). applyFilters() re-copies and re-sorts allLeads on every keystroke/filter change (732, 760-791) and the table renders every row (1140) with no windowing.
- **Problem:** Fine at a few hundred leads, but a busy multi-channel studio accumulating thousands will see slow search-as-you-type, large initial payloads, and heavy re-renders. It also means status counts and the action queue recompute over the full array on each change.
- **Recommendation:** Keep the snappy client-side UX for small workspaces but add server-side pagination + search params (the query already supports status/platform/assigned filters) and switch to fetching a page when allLeads exceeds a threshold; or virtualize the table rows. Debounce the search input as a quick first step (S).

**`platform-12` — WhatsApp status polls every 2 seconds indefinitely, even when fully connected**  
MED · S effort · low impact · performance · standard: Performance · _Platform Plumbing (entitlements, pricing, plan UX, notifications, confirm, AI copilots, trash, WhatsApp, API client)_  
- **Evidence:** app/whatsapp/page.js:22 `const interval = setInterval(fetchStatus, 2000)` with no clear condition; fetchStatus (line 26) hits /api/whatsapp/status with cache:'no-store' every 2s for as long as the tab is open, regardless of status==='connected'.
- **Problem:** Once connected there is nothing to poll for; a 2s no-store poll wastes server/network for an idle page. Tight polling is only needed while a QR is pending.
- **Recommendation:** Back off the interval: poll at 2s only while status is qr_ready/initializing, then drop to 15-30s (or stop) once connected; clear on unmount (already returned). Prefer the SSE /api/events channel the rest of the app uses for status pushes.

**`contracts-13` — Analytics page has no date range, no export, and a single hardcoded funnel**  
LOW · M effort · low impact · ux · standard: Analytics / Exports · _Contracts Studio_  
- **Evidence:** analytics/page.js renders fixed all-time stats and a 3-stage funnel with no time filter, no comparison period, and no export control; backend /cs/analytics (contracts-studio.js:826-850) computes lifetime aggregates only.
- **Problem:** Owners can't answer "how did contracts perform this month vs last" or export the funnel for a report — the analytics are a static snapshot. This is polish, not a blocker, but it limits the page's usefulness for the maturity push.
- **Recommendation:** Add a date-range selector (this month / last 30d / custom) threaded into the analytics query, and an Export button (the app already has an ExportButton component in components/control). Reuse existing patterns.

**`studio-library-9` — Decisive culling persists one HTTP PUT per photo with no batching or offline buffering**  
LOW · M effort · low impact · performance · _Media Studio — Library, Cull & AI_  
- **Evidence:** cull/page.js:184-190 decide() does applyLocal then `await mediaAPI.cullAsset(target.id, { decision })` — one PUT per keystroke. Rapid culling (P/X held down through a 1000-photo shoot) fires ~1000 sequential PUTs. The optimistic local update is good, but there is no debounce/queue, and a transient network blip silently swallows the write (catch {} at :189) with no retry, risking lost decisions.
- **Problem:** Fast culling is the core loop; at speed it generates a request storm and any dropped request silently loses a decision (the UI shows the local optimistic state, so the user never knows). Both a scalability and a data-integrity concern on flaky connections.
- **Recommendation:** Buffer decisions and flush via the existing /cull/bulk endpoint (debounced, e.g. every 500ms or N decisions), with a small retry + a subtle 'saving...' indicator and an error toast on failure. Reuses mediaAPI.bulkCull. Keeps the instant optimistic UX while making persistence robust and cheap.

### Backend data-layer performance (indexes, queries, pragmas)

**`backend-perf-3` — messages.wa_message_id has no index — dedup full-scans on every inbound message and in a 200-row sync loop**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** server.js:624 adds the column (ALTER TABLE messages ADD COLUMN wa_message_id TEXT) but the only messages index is idx_messages_lead ON messages(lead_id) (server.js:757). The dedup key wa_message_id is queried with no index in two write hot paths: whatsapp-service.js:365 'SELECT id FROM messages WHERE wa_message_id = ?' on every inbound WhatsApp message, and server.js:1709 the same query INSIDE the per-message loop of /api/leads/:leadId/messages/sync (loop at 1706, fetchHistory pulls up to 200 messages at 1702).
- **Problem:** Each duplicate check is a full table scan of messages. For the sync endpoint that is up to 200 sequential full scans per lead-open (a true N+1); for live inbound it is one full scan per received message. As the messages table grows (the largest write-volume table), both paths degrade linearly and stall the request/worker thread.
- **Recommendation:** Add CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_messages_wa ON messages(wa_message_id) WHERE wa_message_id IS NOT NULL. Then drop the per-row SELECT entirely and switch the inserts at server.js:1712 and whatsapp-service.js:373 to INSERT OR IGNORE, letting the unique index enforce dedup in a single statement.

**`backend-perf-4` — ms_assets (largest table) indexed only by project_id — workspace/sync/storage scans miss the index**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** media-studio.js:149 — the only ms_assets index is idx_ms_assets_project ON ms_assets(project_id). Multiple workspace-scoped queries cannot use it: sync.js:26 'SELECT * FROM ms_assets WHERE workspace_id=? AND updated_at>? ORDER BY updated_at' (offline-first delta, runs frequently); media-studio.js:550 storage byType 'WHERE workspace_id=? AND deleted_at IS NULL GROUP BY type'; media-studio.js:906 trash sweep 'WHERE deleted_at IS NOT NULL AND deleted_at < ...'; command-center.js:337 per-workspace 'SUM(a.size_bytes) FROM ms_assets WHERE a.workspace_id=w.id'.
- **Problem:** All of these full-scan the highest-row-count table (one row per imported photo/video, easily tens of thousands per studio). The sync delta in particular runs on every reconnect/poll of the desktop and web offline layer, so a slow scan there is felt constantly.
- **Recommendation:** Add two indexes: CREATE INDEX idx_ms_assets_ws_updated ON ms_assets(workspace_id, updated_at) to cover the sync delta + ORDER BY, and CREATE INDEX idx_ms_assets_ws_deleted ON ms_assets(workspace_id, deleted_at) to cover storage rollups and the trash sweep. These directly serve the queries above with no code change.

**`backend-perf-1` — auth middleware runs 4 DB queries on every authenticated request**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** server.js:158-206 — the auth() middleware that guards essentially every /api route runs four sequential better-sqlite3 queries per request: SELECT workspace_id,business_name,full_name FROM users (171), SELECT role,permissions FROM workspace_members (177), SELECT user_id FROM workspace_members WHERE role='super_admin' (182), and SELECT status FROM workspaces (195). Additionally getWorkspaceId() (server.js:2696-2699) re-runs a 5th 'SELECT workspace_id FROM users' inside chat handlers (called at 2718) even though req.workspaceId is already populated by auth.
- **Problem:** Because auth is on the hot path of every single API call, these 4 queries multiply across the entire app. A page that fires 8 parallel API calls on load executes 32 of these lookups before any real work happens. The role lookup and owner lookup hit the same workspace_members table twice; the data is also effectively static per session yet re-fetched every request.
- **Recommendation:** Collapse the user+role+owner lookups into one query (e.g. a single SELECT joining users to workspace_members for the caller's role, plus a cached owner/status). Add a short-lived in-process cache keyed by userId+workspaceId (e.g. 30-60s TTL Map, invalidated on member/plan/suspend mutations) so repeated requests in a burst skip the DB entirely. Delete the redundant getWorkspaceId() call in the chat handlers and use req.workspaceId directly (it is already set at server.js:173).

**`backend-perf-2` — Photo-cull asset grid: 6 correlated subqueries per row over up to 10,000 rows**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** media-studio.js:843-875 — GET /api/media/projects/:id/assets sets limit = Math.min(parseInt(limit)||100, 10000) (848, comment: 'high cap for full-shoot culling') then selects each asset with 6 inline correlated subqueries against ms_asset_scores (lines 860-865: sharpness, quality, exposure, high_clip, shadow_clip, dup_group), each '(SELECT value FROM ms_asset_scores s WHERE s.asset_id=a.id AND s.score_type=? LIMIT 1)'. The only supporting index is idx_ms_scores_asset ON ms_asset_scores(asset_id) (media-studio.js:151) — no (asset_id, score_type) composite.
- **Problem:** On a full-shoot cull view (the explicit 10k use-case), this executes up to 60,000 correlated subquery seeks in a single request, each seeking by asset_id then filtering score_type without a composite index. This is the slowest read path in Media Studio and it is exactly the screen the product wants to feel instant.
- **Recommendation:** Replace the 6 subqueries with one LEFT JOIN + conditional aggregation pivot: JOIN ms_asset_scores once and use MAX(CASE WHEN score_type='quality' THEN value END) AS quality, ... GROUP BY a.id. Add CREATE INDEX idx_ms_scores_asset_type ON ms_asset_scores(asset_id, score_type) so the join is covered. This turns ~60k seeks into one indexed scan of the relevant score rows.

**`backend-perf-5` — No busy_timeout / synchronous pragmas despite a concurrent in-process write worker**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** server.js:38-41 opens the DB and sets only db.pragma('journal_mode = WAL'). A grep across backend/ finds no busy_timeout, synchronous, cache_size, or mmap_size pragma anywhere (only scripts/clean_and_seed_demo.js sets foreign_keys). Meanwhile media-worker.js:873-882 runs a setInterval loop that writes to the same DB (ms_jobs claim at 818, asset UPDATEs throughout) concurrently with request-thread writes.
- **Problem:** Under WAL, the default synchronous=FULL adds extra fsyncs versus the recommended synchronous=NORMAL. More importantly, with no busy_timeout a write that collides with the background worker's write throws SQLITE_BUSY immediately instead of briefly waiting, surfacing as random 500s during media import/export bursts.
- **Recommendation:** Right after WAL is enabled (server.js:41) add db.pragma('synchronous = NORMAL'); db.pragma('busy_timeout = 5000'); (and optionally cache_size = -16000 for a 16MB page cache). All are safe, additive, single-line changes that remove a class of intermittent BUSY failures and reduce write latency.

**`backend-perf-6` — broadcastToWorkspace runs a members query per event, including per ffmpeg progress tick**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** server.js:849-853 — broadcastToWorkspace() runs 'SELECT user_id FROM workspace_members WHERE workspace_id=?' on every call, then fans out. It is invoked 71 times across modules (booking, comms, contracts, media-studio, media-worker, etc.). The worst case is media-worker.js:798: inside runFfmpeg onProgress it calls broadcastToWorkspace(...,'ms_video_export',{progress:pct}) on every progress tick, and media-worker.js:380/475/687/704 fire it once per processed asset during bulk ingest.
- **Problem:** Every realtime event pays a DB round-trip just to resolve recipients. A single video export emits dozens-to-hundreds of progress ticks, each running a members lookup; a bulk photo import of N assets runs N members lookups. The member list changes rarely, so this is repeated wasted work on the realtime path.
- **Recommendation:** Cache the workspace member-id list in a Map (TTL ~60s, or invalidate on member add/remove via the existing member mutation handlers) and have broadcastToWorkspace read from it. Separately, throttle the ffmpeg progress broadcast in media-worker.js:796-799 (e.g. only emit when pct changes by >=2% or every ~750ms) to cut tick volume regardless.

**`backend-perf-8` — /api/cs/overview sums revenue by loading every signed contract's totals into JS**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** contracts-studio.js:443 — inside GET /api/cs/overview (432-447) revenue is computed by db.prepare("SELECT totals FROM cs_documents WHERE workspace_id=? AND status IN ('signed','completed')").all(ws).forEach(r => { revenue += Number(J(r.totals,{}).total||0); }). totals is a JSON text column parsed per row in JS.
- **Problem:** The contracts dashboard loads every signed/completed document row and JSON-parses each just to produce one revenue number. It grows linearly with closed contracts and runs on every dashboard open, when the other tiles on the same handler already use efficient GROUP BY aggregates (e.g. byStatus at 436).
- **Recommendation:** Persist the contract total into a dedicated numeric column (e.g. total_amount REAL) at sign/complete time, then compute revenue with a single SELECT SUM(total_amount) ... WHERE status IN ('signed','completed'). Backfill once from the existing totals JSON. This removes the per-row parse and matches the aggregate style of the rest of the overview.

**`backend-perf-9` — Public gallery portal inserts an access row on every GET with no rate limiting**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · performance · _performance_  
- **Evidence:** media-studio.js:2588-2634 GET /api/media/portal/:token is unauthenticated and on every successful load inserts into ms_gallery_access (2603-2604: 'INSERT INTO ms_gallery_access (...) VALUES (...,CURRENT_TIMESTAMP)'). It also loads all gallery assets unbounded (2598-2601) plus several extra queries (favCounts 2596, proofing 2608-2610, store check 2613, folders 2619) per request.
- **Problem:** Each refresh, bot crawl, or repeated client visit appends a row, so ms_gallery_access grows unbounded with no dedup/throttle, and every hit on a public link runs ~6 queries with no rate limit — an easy amplification target on a surface that faces the open internet.
- **Recommendation:** Throttle the access-row write (e.g. upsert one row per token per session/day instead of per request, or only record when access_token is newly seen) and apply the same lightweight rate limit used on other public token routes. Keep the asset load but consider paginating very large galleries for first paint.

**`galleries-8` — Album editor load issues an N+1 query per slot**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · performance · standard: No N+1 in list/detail loads · _Galleries, Albums & Portfolio (client delivery)_  
- **Evidence:** GET /api/media/albums/:id (media-studio.js:1846-1853) iterates pages, then for every filled slot runs a separate 'SELECT ... FROM ms_assets WHERE id = ?'. A 40-page album with 4-up grids issues ~160 single-row queries on every load and every refetch (the editor refetches after each page add/layout/reorder — studio/[id]/albums/[albumId]/page.js:87-94).
- **Problem:** Bounded but wasteful; combined with the editor's full refetch-on-every-mutation pattern it makes album editing feel sluggish on larger albums.
- **Recommendation:** Collect all slot asset_ids across pages, fetch them in one 'WHERE id IN (...)' query, and map in memory. Optionally switch the editor's mutations to optimistic local updates (it already does this for slots at :66) and drop the full refetch on layout/reorder.

**`studio-video-12` — Auto-reel and AI ranking ignore soft-deleted assets inconsistently**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · data-integrity · standard: Data integrity / consistency · _Media Studio — Video & Reel_  
- **Evidence:** reel-engine.js reel-plan reads assets WITHOUT a deleted_at filter (line 129: 'WHERE project_id=? AND workspace_id=?'), while reel-render (line 146) and video-ai.js videoScores (line 84) and template auto-pick all DO filter deleted_at IS NULL. So the advisory plan can include trashed assets that then vanish at render.
- **Problem:** The reel-plan preview can show counts/segments built from deleted media, diverging from what reel-render actually produces — a subtle correctness/consistency bug.
- **Recommendation:** Add 'AND deleted_at IS NULL' to the reel-plan asset query (reel-engine.js:129) to match every other selection path.

### Security, auth-scoping & money-rail integrity

**`platform-1` — /api/whatsapp/status and /api/whatsapp/disconnect have NO auth and return global, workspace-agnostic state**  
CRIT · M effort · high impact · data-integrity · standard: Permissions / Plan Enforcement · _Platform Plumbing (entitlements, pricing, plan UX, notifications, confirm, AI copilots, trash, WhatsApp, API client)_  
- **Evidence:** backend/server.js:2571 `app.get('/api/whatsapp/status', (req,res)=>res.json(whatsappService.getLegacyStatus()))` and server.js:2595 `app.post('/api/whatsapp/disconnect', ...)` both lack the `auth` middleware that every per-account route (server.js:2574-2592) has. getLegacyStatus (whatsapp-service.js:1035) returns the `__legacy__` instance or the first instance of ANY workspace. app/whatsapp/page.js:28 fetches /api/whatsapp/status with NO Authorization header and polls it every 2s (page.js:22).
- **Problem:** Any unauthenticated caller can read a workspace's WhatsApp connection status (incl. phoneNumber, QR data URL) and disconnect WhatsApp. In a multi-tenant SaaS this is a cross-tenant data leak + denial of service. The page also leaks because the status it shows is not scoped to the logged-in workspace.
- **Recommendation:** Add `auth` to both routes and scope them to req.workspaceId. Better: repoint app/whatsapp/page.js onto the existing per-account routes (/api/whatsapp/accounts/:id/status|connect|disconnect, server.js:2574-2592) which are already auth'd + workspace-scoped, and retire getLegacyStatus once migrated.

**`invoices-store-3` — Manual 'Mark as Paid' bypasses the payments rail — no money record, no audit, no timeline**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · data-integrity · _Invoices, Payments & Store_  
- **Evidence:** invoices/page.js:352-359 handleMarkPaid -> invoicesAPI.update(id,{status:'paid'}) -> PUT /api/invoices/:id (server.js:2229-2243) which contains no logAudit, no addContactHistory, and no payments insert. Contrast payments.js markPaid (70-74)+settle (63-69) which write contact history and broadcast payment_paid.
- **Problem:** Two ways to record payment produce inconsistent data: paying via the pay link creates a payments row + activity-timeline entry + realtime event; clicking 'Mark as Paid' in-app creates none of these. Reconciliation, the activity timeline, audit trail, and any future revenue reporting will silently miss all manually-marked invoices (likely the majority while Stripe is off).
- **Recommendation:** Route manual mark-paid through the payments rail: have the button call paymentsAPI.link({kind:'invoice', ref_id, ...}) then mark-paid (or a dedicated /api/invoices/:id/mark-paid that inserts a manual payments row and calls settle), so every paid invoice has a payment record + audit + history.

**`command-center-2` — Destructive cross-tenant actions use window.prompt/alert with no confirm and no step-up**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · ux · _Command Center (internal platform control plane — /control + /api/cc/*)_  
- **Evidence:** customers/[id]:44 suspend via prompt('Reason for suspension?'); :45 grace via prompt('Grace period days?'); :112-118 add-override via THREE sequential prompts (kind, key, value); :36-39 plan change fires instantly on <select> onChange with no confirmation. flags/page.js:20,59,60 and plans/page.js:31,36,37,38 likewise build flags/limits/features via prompt(). Step-up (ccAuth.stepUp) is wired ONLY in database/page.js:45-53.
- **Problem:** Native prompts are inconsistent with the rest of the app, can't be styled/validated, lose input on misclick, and offer no confirmation gate. A founder can suspend a tenant or flip a plan with one click and no second factor, while the far-less-destructive SQL SELECT requires step-up. This is both a UX-cohesion and a safety inversion.
- **Recommendation:** Reuse the Modal/Field pattern that already exists in support/page.js (lines 279-301) for suspend-with-reason, grace, and the override builder; replace the bare plan <select> with a confirm step. Gate suspend/plan/impersonate/override behind the existing step-up flow used by the SQL console for consistent high-stakes friction.

**`invoices-store-7` — Print orders capture money but never collect it (no pay link / payment)**  
HIGH · L effort · high impact · integration · _Invoices, Payments & Store_  
- **Evidence:** print-store.js POST /api/store/public/:token (96-131) inserts ms_print_orders at status 'new' and messages 'We'll be in touch to finalize it' (127); it never creates a payments row or pay link. payments.js settle() supports kind 'print_order' (66) but nothing triggers it for shop orders.
- **Problem:** The 'store' is order-capture only, not commerce. Even when Stripe is enabled, a customer placing a shop order cannot pay; the studio must chase payment out-of-band, defeating the point of an online store and diverging from the invoice pay-link rail.
- **Recommendation:** On order placement, create a payments row (kind 'print_order', ref_id = order id) and return its pay URL; show 'Pay now' in the /shop done state and surface the pay link to the studio in studio/store orders. This unifies print orders onto the same money rail as invoices.

**`platform-5` — WhatsApp page manages a single legacy connection though the plan sells 2-5 accounts; modern per-account API unused**  
HIGH · L effort · high impact · integration · standard: Plan Enforcement / Consistency · _Platform Plumbing (entitlements, pricing, plan UX, notifications, confirm, AI copilots, trash, WhatsApp, API client)_  
- **Evidence:** entitlements.js limits grant whatsapp_accounts: 1 (creator), 2 (studio), 5 (studio_plus), -1 (enterprise) (lines 98-114). app/whatsapp/page.js only ever calls /api/whatsapp/status|reconnect|disconnect (the legacy single-instance routes). The full per-account API exists and is auth'd+scoped (server.js:2574-2592) and whatsappGroupsAPI.readyAccounts (api.js:217) and platformAccountsAPI (api.js:208) already model multiple accounts.
- **Problem:** Studio/Studio+ customers who pay for multiple WhatsApp numbers have no UI to add or manage them here; the page presents WhatsApp as one global connection. This is both a sold-but-unreachable entitlement and an inconsistency with how Instagram/Facebook accounts are managed via platform-accounts.
- **Recommendation:** Convert app/whatsapp/page.js to a per-account list: read platformAccountsAPI.getAll('whatsapp'), render one status card per account using /api/whatsapp/accounts/:id/status, and an "Add WhatsApp account" CTA gated by atLimit('whatsapp_accounts'). Reuse PlanLock when at limit.

**`booking-4` — Per-service duration is ignored when computing public slots — enables overlapping double-bookings**  
HIGH · S effort · medium impact · data-integrity · _Booking & Scheduling_  
- **Evidence:** The public GET calls computeSlots(row.workspace_id, cfg) WITHOUT the third serviceDuration arg (booking.js:130), so dur falls back to cfg.slot_min/30 (booking.js:64). The manage GET correctly passes b.duration_min (booking.js:180). Slot generation steps by slot_min and the free() overlap check uses `dur` (booking.js:71,80), so a 90-min service is offered every 30 min and validated as if it were 30 min.
- **Problem:** Two clients can book a 90-minute session 30 minutes apart; the studio is double-booked. The single-time clash check on POST (booking.js:143 — exact start_at match only) does not catch overlap either. For a photo/video studio where sessions are long, this is a real scheduling-integrity bug.
- **Recommendation:** In the public GET, compute per-service slot sets: either pass the selected service's duration (the frontend already knows the chosen service) by accepting a ?service= query param, or return a slots map keyed by service. The overlap logic in computeSlots already handles duration correctly once the right value is passed; this is mostly a one-line wiring fix plus a frontend re-fetch on service change.

**`invoices-store-13` — Stripe webhook is unverified (no signature check)**  
HIGH · M effort · medium impact · data-integrity · _Invoices, Payments & Store_  
- **Evidence:** payments.js:117-128 POST /api/payments/webhook trusts req.body, finds the payment by client_reference_id and marks it paid; the comment at 115-116 admits 'production should verify Stripe-Signature ... wire that when enabling Stripe.'
- **Problem:** Anyone who can reach the endpoint and guess/observe a payment id can POST a fake checkout.session.completed and mark an invoice/order paid, falsifying revenue and settling invoices without real payment. This is a money-integrity hole that ships dormant until Stripe is enabled.
- **Recommendation:** Verify Stripe-Signature against STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET on the raw body before markPaid, and reject unverified events. Block enabling Stripe until this is wired.

**`crm-clients-9` — Invoices/lead-detail invoice queries are user_id-scoped, not workspace_id-scoped — risk of missing invoices for team-created records**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · architecture · _CRM — Clients & Contacts_  
- **Evidence:** server.js:2182 GET /api/invoices uses 'WHERE user_id = ?' (req.workspaceOwnerId); lead detail invoices at :1816 and :1748 same. Leads themselves are correctly workspace_id-scoped (server.js:1496). Mixed scoping model within one module.
- **Problem:** Invoices are keyed to the workspace OWNER's user_id rather than workspace_id. If invoice creation ever records a team member's user_id (not the owner's), those invoices vanish from both the Invoices list and the client profile — an inconsistency with the workspace-scoped leads model and a latent data-visibility bug as teams grow.
- **Recommendation:** Standardize invoice reads to workspace scope (add/confirm a workspace_id column on invoices and filter by req.workspaceId), matching the leads model. At minimum, audit the invoice INSERT to guarantee user_id is always the owner. Verify before changing to stay backward-compatible.

**`invoices-store-15` — Pay link is a manual clipboard button, never embedded in the invoice handoff**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · ux · _Invoices, Payments & Store_  
- **Evidence:** InvoiceViewModal makePayLink (118-124) creates a payment link and copies it to clipboard with a transient '✓ Link copied' label; it is not added to the email body or the printed invoice. The send modal message (263-265) contains no pay link.
- **Problem:** Getting paid requires the studio to manually make a link, copy it, and paste it somewhere — and there is no record on the invoice that a link was issued. The 'create -> send -> pay' loop is not closed even ignoring the broken email.
- **Recommendation:** Auto-mint (or reuse) the invoice's pay link when sending and embed a 'Pay now' button in the emailed/printed invoice; persist the link on the invoice so the modal shows its status.

**`invoices-store-9` — Store auto-enables on ALL galleries with no per-gallery opt-out**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · data-integrity · _Invoices, Payments & Store_  
- **Evidence:** media-studio.js:2613 storeEnabled = COUNT(active ms_print_products) > 0 for the workspace; this single boolean drives the 'Order prints' button on every public gallery (g/[token]:202).
- **Problem:** The moment a studio adds one active product, every published gallery (including private/personal or pre-paid deliveries where upselling is inappropriate) shows 'Order prints'. There is no per-gallery toggle, which can embarrass studios in front of clients and is surprising behavior.
- **Recommendation:** Add a per-gallery store_enabled setting (default off or inherit) and AND it with the workspace product check, surfaced in gallery settings next to download/watermark/proofing toggles.

**`invoices-store-10` — No payments overview UI despite a built API**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · integration · _Invoices, Payments & Store_  
- **Evidence:** api.js:550-553 exposes paymentsAPI.list and paymentsAPI.markPaid; backend GET /api/payments (payments.js:91) returns up to 200 payments. No page in wappflow-web/src consumes paymentsAPI.list or markPaid (grep returns no callers).
- **Problem:** There is a money ledger (payments table across invoices/print/booking/contract deposits) with no surface. Studios cannot see all incoming payments in one place, mark a manual payment paid from a central view, or reconcile — fragmenting the 'one OS' money story.
- **Recommendation:** Add a lightweight Payments tab (in Invoices or a Billing section) listing payments with kind/status/amount/date and a Mark Paid action for manual ones, reusing the existing API.

**`comms-14` — Anyone can delete any channel; no permissions/admin UI**  
MED · L effort · medium impact · standards · _Communications (Team Chat + Project Rooms + Huddles/Calls + Floating WhatsApp Chat)_  
- **Evidence:** chatAPI.deleteChannel(id) is exposed (api.js:261) hitting DELETE /api/chat/channels/:id; the create/delete have no role check surfaced and the UI has no channel-member management, no archive, no owner concept. chat_members exists but there's no add/remove-members screen for private channels (only DM auto-membership and self-join on room open).
- **Problem:** In a multi-seat studio (team_collaboration plan), a junior member deleting a channel is destructive and unauditable (no audit log, no recycle bin). Private channels can't have members managed from the UI, limiting real team use.
- **Recommendation:** Add a channel settings popover (rename, archive instead of delete, manage members for private channels) gated by workspace role, and log channel lifecycle events via notify()/an audit table. Archive+restore covers the missing recycle-bin standard cheaply.

**`booking-10` — Timezone is a free-text display label only — slots are server-local with no real TZ handling**  
MED · L effort · medium impact · data-integrity · _Booking & Scheduling_  
- **Evidence:** DEFAULTS.timezone is '' described as 'display label … slots are studio-local' (booking.js:53); the settings UI is a free-text input placeholder 'Asia/Karachi' (bookings/page.js:98). computeSlots builds times with new Date()/setHours in the SERVER's local time (booking.js:74-84) and stores naive 'YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS' strings. The public page just appends the label 'Times shown in {tz}' (book/[slug]/page.js:94) and parses start_at with .replace(' ','T') as browser-local.
- **Problem:** If the server, studio, and client are in different timezones, the displayed times are wrong and there is no DST correctness — the 'timezone' field is cosmetic. For a SaaS that may run cloud-hosted with clients across regions, bookings can land at the wrong real time. It silently 'works' only when server == studio == client TZ.
- **Recommendation:** Treat timezone as a real IANA zone (dropdown, not free text) and compute slots in that zone (e.g. with a TZ-aware date lib), storing UTC or zone-qualified timestamps. This is larger; flag it now so it's fixed before multi-region scale rather than after bad bookings occur.

**`invoices-store-14` — Shop checkout has no payment id idempotency / over-clickable place-order**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · data-integrity · _Invoices, Payments & Store_  
- **Evidence:** shop/[token]/page.js place() (31-36) sets busy=true but on error sets busy=false; the success path leaves busy true. The button (107) is disabled while busy, but there is no server-side dedupe — print-store.js POST creates a new ms_print_orders row every call with no idempotency key.
- **Problem:** A double network submit (e.g. retry, slow connection) can create duplicate print orders and duplicate CRM contact-history + WhatsApp confirmations, since the server has no idempotency guard.
- **Recommendation:** Generate a client idempotency token per checkout session and dedupe server-side (unique constraint or recent-duplicate check) before insert in print-store.js.

**`settings-team-15` — Permission editors allow free-text custom permission keys the backend likely does not enforce**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · data-integrity · _Settings, Team, Onboarding & Knowledge_  
- **Evidence:** team/page.js MemberPermissionsModal addCustomPerm (line 266) and PermissionsTab addCustomPerm (line 368) let an admin type any key (e.g. 'export_data'), normalize to snake_case, and persist via updateMember/updateRolePermissions. PERM_LABELS (lines 23-33) defines only 9 known permissions; custom keys are stored but nothing indicates the app reads them.
- **Problem:** Admins can create arbitrary permission toggles implying capabilities the app never checks (toggling a self-invented 'export_data' off does nothing), producing a false sense of access control.
- **Recommendation:** Either remove the free-text custom-permission feature, or label custom keys 'inactive until wired up', and validate keys against a backend-known registry so admins cannot define unenforced permissions.

### Stale plan/pricing vocabulary & currency

**`command-center-3` — Stale plan vocabulary (free/starter/growth) across CC contradicts the live pricing engine (creator/studio/studio_plus)**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · consistency · _Command Center (internal platform control plane — /control + /api/cc/*)_  
- **Evidence:** Live plan keys per entitlements.js:119 are creator/studio/studio_plus/enterprise. But customers/page.js:40 hard-codes filter options free/starter/growth/enterprise; planTone maps free/starter/growth in customers/page.js:93, page.js:63, health/page.js:60, and timemachine/page.js:182.
- **Problem:** The Customers plan filter offers values that match NO real workspace, so filtering by plan silently returns zero rows. Plan pills across Overview/Customers/Health/TimeMachine fall through to the neutral tone for every real plan (creator/studio/studio_plus), making the colour coding meaningless. This is a visible correctness/consistency bug.
- **Recommendation:** Drive plan options and tones from the live catalog (ccApi.plans() already returns keys + names) instead of hard-coded lists. Centralize one planTone()/planLabel() helper in ccApi.js and import it everywhere so plan vocabulary stays in sync with entitlements.js.

**`settings-team-2` — Upgrade overlays on Team & Knowledge say required plan is 'Growth' - no such plan exists**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · terminology · _Settings, Team, Onboarding & Knowledge_  
- **Evidence:** team/page.js line 568 requiredPlan="Growth" with perks 'Up to 5 (Growth) or unlimited (Enterprise)'; knowledge/page.js line 245 requiredPlan="Growth". plan.js plan keys are studio/studio_plus/enterprise and line 96 maps isGrowth: plan==='studio'. NavBar.js lines 182-184 correctly gate the same features with requiredPlan:'Studio'. The pricing catalog is Creator/Studio/Studio+/Enterprise - no 'Growth' tier.
- **Problem:** A locked-out free user is told to upgrade to 'Growth', a plan absent from the Plan & Billing tab (which lists Studio/Studio+/Enterprise). A self-contradicting upgrade ask erodes trust and blocks conversion.
- **Recommendation:** Replace the hardcoded 'Growth' strings in team/page.js (568) and knowledge/page.js (245) with 'Studio' to match NavBar and the plan catalog, and fix the perks copy. Better: source requiredPlan from a shared TAB_GATING-style constant so it cannot drift again.

**`command-center-4` — Overview 'Implied MRR/ARR' and Plans pricing render PKR amounts with a $ symbol**  
HIGH · S effort · medium impact · data-integrity · _Command Center (internal platform control plane — /control + /api/cc/*)_  
- **Evidence:** entitlements.js:119 PLAN_MONTHLY_PRICE is in PKR (creator:7999, studio:14999, studio_plus:29999) with PLAN_CURRENCY (PKR). command-center.js:289-291 sums those into impliedMrr. The UI formats it with fmtMoney = `$${...}` (ccApi.js:110) at page.js:20-21. plans/page.js:38,80 hard-codes USD and a literal '$' for price entry/display.
- **Problem:** A founder reads 'Implied MRR $14,999' when the underlying figure is PKR — a ~280x currency mislabel that makes the single most important executive number wrong. The Plans editor also forces new prices into USD, conflicting with the PKR pricing engine.
- **Recommendation:** Pass currency through from the plan catalog and format MRR/ARR and plan prices with the actual PLAN_CURRENCY (PKR). Keep $ only for genuinely USD figures (AI provider cost, Cloudflare R2 invoice). A currency-aware fmtMoney(amount, currency) avoids future drift.

**`platform-9` — Plan optimistic-unlock can briefly expose gated UI and flash locked features after load**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · ux · standard: Plan Enforcement / Loading States · _Platform Plumbing (entitlements, pricing, plan UX, notifications, confirm, AI copilots, trash, WhatsApp, API client)_  
- **Evidence:** lib/plan.js:49-53 hasFeature returns true while planInfo is null ('optimistic-unlocked until the plan loads'); hasLimit (line 56-64) and usageLevel (line 68) likewise default to unlimited/ok. PlanProvider fetches on mount with a 5-min refresh (plan.js:41-45).
- **Problem:** On a slow plan-info fetch, gated buttons/sections render as unlocked first paint, then lock — a visible flash and a momentary window where a user can click into a feature they do not have. The comment acknowledges the trade-off but there is no skeleton/disabled state during `loading`.
- **Recommendation:** Expose `loading` (already in context, plan.js:81) to gate-consuming components and render a neutral disabled/skeleton state while loading instead of optimistic-unlocked, or default high-value gates to locked-until-known. Small change, removes the flash.

**`platform-10` — Inconsistent upgrade destination: UsageWarnings -> ?tab=plan, all PlanLock CTAs -> ?tab=workspace**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · consistency · standard: Plan Enforcement / Consistency · _Platform Plumbing (entitlements, pricing, plan UX, notifications, confirm, AI copilots, trash, WhatsApp, API client)_  
- **Evidence:** UsageWarnings.js:66 `router.push('/settings?tab=plan')`. PlanLock.js LockedOverlay:76, UpgradeCta:91, PlanBanner:145, PlanChip:169 all `router.push('/settings?tab=workspace')`. NavBar.js:345 nav-lock also pushes '/settings?tab=plan'.
- **Problem:** Two different settings tabs are used as 'the upgrade screen' depending on which component fired. Users land on different pages for the same intent (upgrade), and one of the tabs may not even be the billing/plan tab — fragmenting the upgrade funnel.
- **Recommendation:** Pick one canonical plan/billing route (likely ?tab=plan, matching UsageWarnings + NavBar) and update all PlanLock CTAs to it. Centralize as a constant (e.g. PLAN_SETTINGS_ROUTE) so it cannot drift again.

**`integration-8` — Main Analytics ignores invoices, contracts and bookings — revenue is lead-estimate only**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · integration · _integration_  
- **Evidence:** reports/overview (server.js:2356-2430) derives revenue exclusively from leads.actual_sale where status='Closed - Won' (lines 2386-2390) and otherwise reports lead pipeline/sources/agent-perf/response-time. It never queries invoices (paid), cs_documents (signed value, contracts-studio.js:443), bookings, or print orders. Contracts and Command Center maintain their own separate analytics.
- **Problem:** The flagship Analytics screen shows pipeline estimates, not real money: a paid invoice or a signed contract whose value wasn't typed into actual_sale is invisible, and there is no single business view across modules. For a maturity push centered on 'one product', the top analytics surface contradicting the billing data is a credibility gap.
- **Recommendation:** Augment reports/overview with invoice-paid revenue (SUM(total) WHERE status='paid') and signed-contract value, shown alongside pipeline revenue, or add a unified 'Business' overview that joins leads + invoices + contracts + bookings. Reuse existing per-module queries; keep additive.

**`platform-14` — isGrowth alias maps to studio, muddying tier checks during the rename transition**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · terminology · standard: Terminology / Consistency · _Platform Plumbing (entitlements, pricing, plan UX, notifications, confirm, AI copilots, trash, WhatsApp, API client)_  
- **Evidence:** lib/plan.js:96 `isGrowth: plan === 'studio'` and lib/plan.js:94-95 hardcode `isFree:false, isStarter:false`. These back-compat aliases coexist with new isCreator/isStudio/isStudioPlus flags.
- **Problem:** Any lingering component still checking isGrowth silently treats Studio as the old Growth tier, and isFree/isStarter being permanently false can mask logic that assumed those tiers. This is latent rename debt that makes tier behavior hard to reason about.
- **Recommendation:** Grep for isGrowth/isFree/isStarter consumers, migrate them to the new flags, then delete the aliases. Keeping them invites silent tier mismatches like the PlanWelcomeModal/PlanLock dead-tier bugs.

### Weak guidance, terminology & first-run affordances

**`crm-leads-3` — Duplicate-on-create returns existing_id but the UI shows a dead-end error instead of linking to the existing lead**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · data-integrity · _CRM — Leads & Pipeline_  
- **Evidence:** Backend POST /api/leads returns `{ error: 'A lead with this phone number already exists', existing_id: existing.id }` (server.js:1842). AddLeadModal catches it and renders only `err.response?.data?.error` (AddLeadModal.js:32) — grep confirms `existing_id` is referenced nowhere in app/ or components/.
- **Problem:** The user hits a wall ('already exists') with no way to act — they can't jump to the existing lead, so they either abandon or create a near-duplicate via a slightly different phone format. The dedupe signal the backend worked to produce is thrown away.
- **Recommendation:** When the create response includes existing_id, replace the error with an inline prompt: 'This phone already belongs to <name> — [Open lead]' that routes to /leads/${existing_id}. Small change, closes the duplicate-prevention loop at the only place it matters.

**`comms-4` — NavBar entry mislabeled 'Inbox' for team chat; module name is inconsistent across the app**  
HIGH · S effort · high impact · terminology · _Communications (Team Chat + Project Rooms + Huddles/Calls + Floating WhatsApp Chat)_  
- **Evidence:** NAV_ITEMS has { label:'Inbox', icon: Inbox, path:'/chat' } (NavBar.js:171). The /chat page header renders 'Team Chat' (page.js:793). The backend/module is 'Communications 2.0' (comms.js:4). Meanwhile customer WhatsApp conversations live in FloatingChat / leads — the thing a CRM user expects 'Inbox' to mean.
- **Problem:** Three names for one destination, and the name chosen ('Inbox') actively mispredicts the content (users expect the customer message inbox, get internal team chat). Breaks the 'one cohesive product' goal at the most visible surface — the primary nav.
- **Recommendation:** Pick one term ('Team' or 'Messages' or 'Communications') and use it in NAV_ITEMS, the page header, and the empty states. Given customer messaging already lives elsewhere, 'Team' or 'Messages' is least ambiguous; keep the Inbox term reserved for customer conversations.

**`crm-leads-2` — New Lead modal can't capture email, source, address, assignee, or tags — forces an immediate second edit**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · ux · _CRM — Leads & Pipeline_  
- **Evidence:** components/AddLeadModal.js:7-13 formData only has customer_name, customer_phone, status, estimated_value, first_message. The detail page (leads/[id]/page.js:1387-1418) and CSV export (leads-list/page.js:883-884) both handle email, lead_source, address, assigned_to and tags, and the backend POST /api/leads accepts email/address/date_of_birth/lead_source (server.js:1837).
- **Problem:** A rep adding a lead from a phone call must create the record, get redirected, open the lead, then inline-edit 3-5 more fields — unnecessary module-jumping for the most frequent capture path. lead_source especially is left blank, degrading later source analytics.
- **Recommendation:** Add optional Email, Lead Source (select from LEAD_SOURCES), and Assignee fields to AddLeadModal (the backend already accepts them). Keep them collapsed under a 'More details' disclosure to preserve the fast path.

**`crm-clients-4` — Invoices page has no 'New invoice' button — primary object can't be created in its own module**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · discoverability · _CRM — Clients & Contacts_  
- **Evidence:** invoices/page.js header has only a 'Back' button (428); empty state text: 'Create invoices from any lead profile.' (491). Creation lives in InvoiceModal inside leads/[id]/page.js:288. No create entry point on /invoices or /clients.
- **Problem:** A first-time user opening Invoices (a top-level nav item, NavBar.js:172) finds a list they cannot add to. Forcing every invoice to start from a lead profile is undiscoverable and adds clicks for the most common action.
- **Recommendation:** Add a 'New invoice' button to the Invoices page header (and to the Clients card / client profile) that opens the existing InvoiceModal with a lead/client picker prefilled. Reuse the InvoiceModal already in leads/[id]/page.js — extract it to a shared component.

**`contracts-5` — No way to send or act on a document from the Overview list — every action forces a builder round-trip**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · ux · standard: Bulk Actions / quick actions · _Contracts Studio_  
- **Evidence:** Overview rows route to /contracts/{id} on click and expose only a delete button (page.js:107-117). Send, remind, copy-link, and duplicate all live exclusively inside the builder toolbar ([id]/page.js:85-93). There is no row-level menu and no multi-select.
- **Problem:** The most common repeat action — resend/remind an awaiting document, or copy its link — requires opening the full builder, waiting for blocks to load, and finding the toolbar button. For an upload-and-sign doc there is no content to edit at all, yet you still must enter the builder to send.
- **Recommendation:** Add a per-row overflow menu (Send, Copy link, Remind, Duplicate, Delete) on the Overview list, and optionally checkboxes for multi-select bulk Remind/Delete/Export. The backend send/remind endpoints already exist; this is mostly wiring + a small menu component reused from the CRM.

**`booking-1` — Owner cannot cancel or reschedule a booking from the admin UI**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · ux · _Booking & Scheduling_  
- **Evidence:** backend/booking.js:119-122 exposes POST /api/booking/:id/cancel and lib/api.js:564 exports bookingAPI.cancel, but app/bookings/page.js never imports or calls it — the only per-row action is 'Open lead →' (bookings/page.js:129). There is no owner-side reschedule endpoint at all (only the public token route at booking.js:183).
- **Problem:** When a client phones the studio to move or cancel a time (the common case), the owner has no in-app path. They must dig into the lead's reminder or message the client manually, and the stale booking still occupies its slot in computeSlots until manually cancelled. This breaks the 'one cohesive product' promise — the operator console is read-only.
- **Recommendation:** Wire bookingAPI.cancel into each row of the Upcoming list (a Cancel button with the existing useConfirm dialog), and add an owner reschedule action that reuses the token-based reschedule flow (or a new authed PUT /api/booking/:id/reschedule). Reuse the same day/time picker component the public page uses to avoid divergence.

**`invoices-store-4` — No way to create an invoice from the Invoices page (forced module-jump to a lead)**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · ux · _Invoices, Payments & Store_  
- **Evidence:** invoices/page.js has no New/Create button anywhere (header at 423-432 has only a Back button; toolbar at 453-473 is search+filters). Creation lives in leads/[id] InvoiceModal (handleSave -> invoicesAPI.create at ~319). Empty state copy at 491 reads 'Create invoices from any lead profile.'
- **Problem:** The dedicated Invoices screen cannot create an invoice. Every invoice requires navigating away to find a lead first, even for ad-hoc/non-lead billing. This fails the 3-question test ('What should I do next?') for a new user landing on an empty invoices page and is a clear friction point versus every invoicing tool.
- **Recommendation:** Add a 'New Invoice' button to the invoices header that opens the same InvoiceModal (lift it into a shared component) with an optional client/lead picker (reusing the client search already in CRM). Keep the lead-profile entry point too.

**`crm-leads-9` — Detail page never surfaces a 'next best action' for the open lead — the guidance only lives on the list**  
MED · M effort · high impact · ux · _CRM — Leads & Pipeline_  
- **Evidence:** The list computes a heuristic nextAction per lead (leads-list/page.js:48-65) and shows the Action Queue. The detail page has the same raw data (status, last_message_at, unread) but renders no equivalent banner; the AI next_action exists only inside the AI tab after a manual click (leads/[id]/page.js:2599-2603).
- **Problem:** A rep who clicks into a lead loses the 'what should I do next' signal they had on the list — the detail page (where they actually act) is the weakest on the third question. This undercuts the cohesion goal: the same intelligence isn't present across screens.
- **Recommendation:** Reuse the nextAction() heuristic (extract it to lib/) and render a one-line action banner in the detail header (e.g. 'Negotiating · quiet 3d → Follow up') with a button that focuses the composer. When AI analysis has run, prefer aiAnalysis.next_action. Pure reuse, no new feature.

**`command-center-5` — Audit Center and Event Stream expose no filters despite backend support**  
HIGH · M effort · medium impact · ux · _Command Center (internal platform control plane — /control + /api/cc/*)_  
- **Evidence:** Backend: /api/cc/audit accepts admin_id/action/target_type/workspace_id (command-center.js:673-680); /api/cc/events accepts type/workspace_id (command-center.js:650-654); ccApi already passes params (ccApi.js:61-62). UI: audit/page.js:11 calls ccApi.audit({limit:200}) with no filter controls; events/page.js:12 calls ccApi.events({limit:150}) with none.
- **Problem:** The Audit Center is the compliance surface yet there's no way to filter by who/what/which-workspace/date — finding a specific action in a capped 200-row flat list is impractical. Same for the Event Stream. The capability exists server-side and is simply not surfaced.
- **Recommendation:** Add a filter bar (admin select, action select, target_type, workspace search, date range) to Audit Center and a type/workspace filter to Events, reusing the select/input styles already defined in customers/page.js. Wire to the existing query params; add pagination beyond the cap.

**`crm-clients-5` — 'Open' and 'Open chat' buttons on a client card go to the exact same route**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · ux · _CRM — Clients & Contacts_
