- **Evidence:** clients/page.js:129 (Open → router.push(`/leads/${c.id}`)) and :130 (MessageCircle 'Open chat' → router.push(`/leads/${c.id}`)) are identical.
- **Problem:** Two visually distinct buttons imply two destinations; they do the same thing. The chat icon promises to deep-link into the conversation but just opens the profile, eroding trust in the UI's affordances.
- **Recommendation:** Either remove the redundant chat button, or deep-link it to the chat tab/scroll of the detail page (e.g. /leads/${c.id}?focus=chat) and have the detail page honor that param so the two buttons differ meaningfully.

**`contracts-6` — Send modal blocks WhatsApp/email when no lead is linked, with no ad-hoc recipient entry**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · ux · standard: UX completeness · _Contracts Studio_  
- **Evidence:** SendModal initializes channels to [] when !hasLead and disables both channel buttons (cursor:'not-allowed', opacity .5) — [id]/page.js:538,555-556. The only path for an unlinked doc is copy-link.
- **Problem:** A common case — sending a one-off proposal to someone who isn't yet a CRM lead — can't use the built-in WhatsApp/email delivery at all; the user must copy the link and paste it into another app, losing tracking attribution. The backend send already accepts a signer with phone/email, so the limitation is purely UI.
- **Recommendation:** When no lead is linked, let the user type a one-off email and/or phone in the Send modal (or auto-create/seed a signer from those values, which the backend already supports at :901-902). This unlocks tracked delivery without forcing a CRM link.

**`contracts-8` — Client Vault is a read-only dead-end — no actions, fails the “what next?” test**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · ux · standard: UX completeness · _Contracts Studio_  
- **Evidence:** vault/page.js renders expandable client cards listing documents (lines 48-84). The only action besides opening a document is "Copy client portal link" (line 69). There is no "New document for this client", no export, no bulk action.
- **Problem:** The Vault is positioned as the per-client hub but offers nothing to DO from there. Having found a client, the user must leave, click New document, and re-link the same client they're already looking at — redundant navigation that contradicts the module's relationship-centric framing.
- **Recommendation:** Add a "New document" button per client card that opens NewDocModal with that lead pre-linked, and a per-client "Export all (PDF/zip)" for signed documents. Reuse NewDocModal from page.js.

**`booking-5` — 'Upcoming' admin list shows past bookings, newest-date-first, with no status badge**  
MED · S effort · medium impact · consistency · _Booking & Scheduling_  
- **Evidence:** booking.js:114 — `SELECT * FROM bookings WHERE workspace_id=? AND status!='cancelled' ORDER BY start_at DESC LIMIT 200` has no `start_at >= now` floor and orders DESC. The page renders this verbatim under an `<h2>Upcoming</h2>` (bookings/page.js:120-127) with the row showing only name·service·date·phone — no status pill, no 'today/tomorrow' grouping.
- **Problem:** The heading says 'Upcoming' but the list is actually 'all non-cancelled bookings, most-recently-dated first', so a booking from last month sits at the top. There is also no visual status (confirmed vs rescheduled) — inconsistent with the manage page which does show a status pill (manage/[token]/page.js:45) and with other list modules in the app that use badges.
- **Recommendation:** Split into 'Upcoming' (start_at >= now, ASC) and a collapsible 'Past'. Add a status badge component consistent with the manage page's pill. Keep it data-driven; this is a query + small render change.

**`crm-leads-4` — No proactive duplicate detection for name/email at capture — only exact phone is blocked, only phone+name are mergeable**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · data-integrity · _CRM — Leads & Pipeline_  
- **Evidence:** findLeadByPhone (server.js:924-942) blocks only digit-normalized phone on create. The reactive duplicates scanner (server.js:1977-1995) groups by normalized phone then by normalized name, but never by email. A website/Instagram lead with no phone but a matching email therefore creates a silent duplicate and is never flagged by the merge tool.
- **Problem:** Multi-channel studios get the same person twice (e.g. an Instagram DM lead and a WhatsApp lead, or two web-form submissions sharing an email). These never surface in Merge Duplicates, fragmenting conversation history and skewing pipeline counts.
- **Recommendation:** Add an email branch to GET /api/leads/duplicates (group by normalized email for leads not already phone/name-grouped) and have AddLeadModal soft-warn (not block) when email matches an existing lead. Reuse the existing MergeDuplicatesModal UI.

**`crm-leads-6` — Three overlapping activity views (Timeline tab + History tab + Activity stats card) confuse rather than clarify**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · consistency · _CRM — Leads & Pipeline_  
- **Evidence:** leads/[id]/page.js defines a 'Timeline' tab (2639) AND a 'History' tab (2134, 'Complete history… preserved forever') AND an 'Activity' stats card in the left rail (1534-1551). Timeline server-side already folds contact_history into itself (server.js:4813-4814), so History is a strict subset of Timeline rendered with a near-identical vertical-line layout.
- **Problem:** Two tabs render the same contact_history rows with different icon maps (HISTORY_ICONS vs TIMELINE_ICONS) and different empty states, and the left card duplicates the counts. Users can't tell which is authoritative — classic module-sprawl.
- **Recommendation:** Collapse History into Timeline (Timeline already supersets it). Keep History only if you add a filter chip ('All / Messages / Status / Notes') to Timeline; otherwise delete the History tab and rename Timeline to 'Activity'. Reduces the tab count from 11 to 10 and removes a duplicate code path.

**`crm-clients-6` — 'Lifetime revenue' on Clients sums actual_sale (one won-deal field), not real invoice/payment totals**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · data-integrity · _CRM — Clients & Contacts_  
- **Evidence:** clients/page.js:52 revenue = clients.reduce((s,c)=>s+Number(c.actual_sale||0)). actual_sale is the single value entered in WonModal at win time (leads/[id]/page.js:236; server.js:1919). Repeat invoices (invoices table) are never added.
- **Problem:** For a studio with repeat clients (the whole point of a Clients module), 'Lifetime revenue' is wrong — it shows only the first deal's value and ignores every subsequent paid invoice, undercounting the client's true worth and making the headline stat untrustworthy.
- **Recommendation:** Compute per-client lifetime value server-side as SUM(invoices.total WHERE status='paid' AND lead_id=...) joined into the client=1 list response, and display that as 'Lifetime revenue' with actual_sale as a fallback. Surface 'amount outstanding' alongside it for actionability.

**`comms-10` — Mentions resolved by fragile name-substring regex — wrong/duplicate/no tags**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · data-integrity · _Communications (Team Chat + Project Rooms + Huddles/Calls + Floating WhatsApp Chat)_  
- **Evidence:** handleSend resolves mentions by testing, for every member, a regex `@<fullname>` against the plain text (page.js:549-552). Names with no full_name fall back to business_name/email; common substrings (e.g. two 'Ali' / 'Ali Khan') match loosely; the @-autocomplete inserts '@'+name (insertMention line 598) but the mention isn't stored as a stable token, so re-parsing relies on exact name text surviving edits.
- **Problem:** Mentions silently mis-target (mentions everyone whose name is a substring) or miss (name with punctuation, or after edit). @channel/@everyone/@here are expanded server-side (comms.js:160) but individual targeting is unreliable, which erodes trust in mentions.
- **Recommendation:** Carry the resolved user_id from the autocomplete selection instead of re-deriving from text: on insertMention, push member.user_id into a ref-backed set and send that as `mentions` (the backend already accepts an explicit id array, afterMessage line 159). Optionally wrap the inserted token in a data-uid span for robust re-parsing on edit.

**`comms-12` — Unbounded message load (no pagination / infinite scroll)**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · performance · _Communications (Team Chat + Project Rooms + Huddles/Calls + Floating WhatsApp Chat)_  
- **Evidence:** loadMessages fetches the latest 60 (page.js:497) and there is no 'load older' control; the GET route supports a `before` cursor (server.js:2760) but the client never uses it. Scroll always jumps to bottom on any messages change (page.js:402-404), so even if older messages were prepended the view would snap down.
- **Problem:** Channel history older than 60 messages is unreachable in the UI, and the unconditional scrollIntoView would fight any back-scroll. For long-running channels this hides history and makes search the only way back.
- **Recommendation:** Add 'Load earlier messages' (or scroll-up trigger) using the existing `before` cursor, and only auto-scroll-to-bottom when the user is already near the bottom (track scroll position) so reading history isn't interrupted by new messages.

**`command-center-9` — Workspace 360 doesn't connect health risk factors to the actions on the same screen**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · ux · _Command Center (internal platform control plane — /control + /api/cc/*)_  
- **Evidence:** command-center.js:378 returns scores but the workspace 360 response does NOT include scores.risk_factors rendering — customers/[id]:59-66 shows only the 4 numeric scores. The risk_factors (inactive_30d, no_ai, low_adoption...) are computed (cc-metering.js:87-93) and shown only on the Health table (health/page.js:67) and Inbox.
- **Problem:** When an admin is on a specific workspace deciding what to do, the 'why is this at risk' signal is on a different page. The Inbox deep-links here but the diagnostic context (risk factors, recent activity-to-action) doesn't travel, forcing module-jumping back to Health.
- **Recommendation:** Render scores.risk_factors as pills in the Workspace 360 Health card (reuse RISK_LABELS from health/page.js) and add a one-click 'Open in Time Machine' / 'Create Support ticket' / 'Add to Inbox' handoff so a risk signal converts directly into an action without leaving the screen.

**`crm-clients-10` — Clients page has no loading skeleton parity and no error state on load failure**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · consistency · _CRM — Clients & Contacts_  
- **Evidence:** clients/page.js:39-43 load() swallows errors (catch {}) and just shows the empty 'No clients yet' state; loading is a plain text line (87). Invoices uses a spinner (invoices/page.js:483) and lead detail has a real fetchError state (leads/[id]/page.js:796).
- **Problem:** A network failure on the Clients page is indistinguishable from genuinely having zero clients, and the loading treatment differs from sibling pages — small but real inconsistency that can mask outages and make the app feel uneven.
- **Recommendation:** Add an explicit error state (retry button) distinct from the empty state, and align the loading affordance with the spinner used on Invoices for a consistent feel across CRM list pages.

**`booking-11` — Admin booking list swallows load errors and has no skeleton/error state**  
LOW · S effort · low impact · ux · _Booking & Scheduling_  
- **Evidence:** bookings/page.js:22 — `bookingAPI.list().then(...).catch(() => {})` discards any failure silently; the list simply renders empty with 'No bookings yet'. The settings load similarly degrades to an empty object (line 21). Only a single text 'Loading…' covers the whole page until settings resolve (page.js:37).
- **Problem:** If the list call fails (auth expiry, server error) the owner sees 'No bookings yet — share your link to get started', which is misleading and could make them think bookings were lost. Inconsistent with modules that show explicit error/retry states.
- **Recommendation:** Surface a small error+retry inline for the list, and a skeleton row while loading, matching the loading/error pattern used elsewhere in the app.

**`command-center-11` — No mobile/responsive handling for the control shell**  
LOW · M effort · low impact · performance · _Command Center (internal platform control plane — /control + /api/cc/*)_  
- **Evidence:** ControlShell.js:64 uses a fixed width:232 sticky sidebar with no collapse/hamburger and no r-* responsive utilities (the rest of wappflow-web adopted an r-* mobile system per MEMORY mobile-responsive note). Wide tables (customers, support, audit, ai recent) have no horizontal-scroll affordance on small screens.
- **Problem:** On a phone/tablet the sidebar consumes a third of the viewport and tables overflow. Founders increasingly triage on mobile (the desktop app even wraps /control), so the control plane is effectively desktop-only.
- **Recommendation:** Add a collapsible sidebar (hamburger under a breakpoint) and wrap data tables in an overflow-x container, reusing the existing r-* responsive conventions from the main app shell for consistency.

### Other / completeness-pass findings

**`gap-1` — Public client pages brand inconsistently and never use the studio's uploaded logo**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · consistency · standard: White-label / brand consistency across the public surface · _completeness_  
- **Evidence:** /d/[token]/page.js:72-77 Brand component hardcodes a 'W' logo on a #0ea5e9→#6366f1 gradient + the document title (no studio name); g/[token]/page.js:269 renders footer 'Delivered with WappFlow Media Studio'; client/[token]/page.js:57-58,101, shop/[token]/page.js:52-53, book/[slug]/page.js:53-54,101 all show {data.brand}[0] first-letter avatar + 'Powered by {brand}'; pay/[token]/page.js has NO brand element at all. Backend brandName() in booking.js:56 returns only company_name, never company_logo, and company_logo exists (server.js:302) with an upload endpoint (server.js:1366).
- **Problem:** A studio's clients see four-to-five different identities across the same studio's links — a WappFlow logo on the contract, a 'Delivered with WappFlow Media Studio' line on the gallery, the studio name elsewhere, and nothing on the payment page. The uploaded company logo is dead: no public page renders it. This is the single most visible break of the 'one cohesive, white-labelable product' goal because it is what the studio's OWN customers see.
- **Recommendation:** Add the studio logo + name + accent color to a shared public-page header component and a shared 'Powered by {studio}' footer; have every public backend endpoint (cs/public, media/portal, store/public, booking/public, payments/public, client-portal/public) return { brand, logo_url, accent } from company_settings; replace the hardcoded 'W' on /d and the 'Delivered with WappFlow' footer on /g; render the logo image when present, fall back to the first-letter avatar only when absent.

**`gap-2` — Zero App Router error/not-found boundaries — any render error shows a raw crash screen**  
HIGH · M effort · high impact · standards · standard: Next.js App Router error & not-found conventions · _completeness_  
- **Evidence:** find over wappflow-web/src/app returns 0 error.js/global-error.js, 0 not-found.js, 0 loading.js. Root layout.js:35-66 wraps children in <Providers> only, with no error boundary. Heavy client god-pages (settings/page.js 3173 lines, leads/[id]/page.js 2969, dashboard 1347) are 'use client' with many array.map renders and no boundary.
- **Problem:** An uncaught exception in any client component (a malformed API row, an undefined .map, a thrown render) takes down the whole route to Next's default error overlay in dev or a blank page in prod, with no branded recovery or retry. There is also no unified not-found UI, so 404s and the various 'missing'/'expired' public states each hand-roll their own copy. This undermines the 'feels like one stable OS' goal the moment anything goes wrong.
- **Recommendation:** Add a root app/error.js and app/global-error.js with a branded 'Something went wrong / Retry' card (reset() button), a shared app/not-found.js, and per-shell error.js under /studio, /contracts, /control. Reuse one ErrorState component so the contract/gallery/pay 'not found / expired' screens render through it too.

**`navigation-ia-4` — Almost no cross-module deep links — the modules don't connect around a contact**  
HIGH · L effort · high impact · integration · _Navigation & Information Architecture_  
- **Evidence:** A grep across app/leads, app/clients, app/dashboard, app/chat for links into /contracts or /studio returns ZERO results. The only cross-module integration that exists is one-directional and partial: leads/[id]/page.js has a LeadStudioSection (548-580) and an InvoiceModal (288-323), and studio/store/page.js:84 links an order back to its lead. There is no 'Create contract for this lead/client' anywhere in CRM, no path from a client to their delivered galleries/contracts, and no unified per-contact timeline. Contracts and Studio do not surface the originating lead/client either (only the store order does).
- **Problem:** The pitch is one operating system around the studio's client relationship, but the modules barely reference each other. To send a contract to a lead you've been chatting with, you leave CRM, open Contracts in a new tab, and manually re-enter the client. This is the central cohesion gap — features exist as silos rather than as one workflow per client.
- **Recommendation:** Add module-action affordances on the lead/client detail screen mirroring the existing LeadStudioSection pattern: a 'Contracts' section (list this contact's contracts + 'New contract' prefilled with their name/phone) and a 'Galleries/Bookings' summary. Reuse the same SidePanel/section components already on leads/[id]. This is additive and turns the lead detail page into the per-client hub that ties the OS together.
- _Reviewer nuance:_ The core cohesion gap is real: NO links into /contracts anywhere in CRM (leads/clients/dashboard/chat), no 'Create contract for this lead', no per-contact contract/gallery hub; the only back-link is store->lead (studio/store/page.js:84, o.lead_id router.push(`/leads/${o.lead_id}`)). BUT the evidence's specific claim that 'a grep ... into /contracts or /studio returns ZERO results' is inaccurate: leads/[id]/page.js:564 and :580 call open('/studio') inside LeadStudioSection. Those are generic links to the Studio root (not deep-linked to the lead's project), so the cohesion argument stands, but the 'zero /studio links' wording is wrong and should be corrected to 'only a generic, non-deep /studio link exists; no /contracts link at all.'

**`gap-5` — Dashboard SSE silently ignores payments, signed contracts, bookings and all Media Studio events**  
MED · M effort · high impact · integration · standard: Unified real-time event bus · _completeness_  
- **Evidence:** dashboard/page.js:678-719 switch handles only lead_created/new_lead, new_message, lead_updated, missed_sync_complete; everything else hits `default: break` (line 718). Backend broadcasts payment_paid (payments.js:73), cs_signed (contracts-studio), booking_created (booking.js:167,195,208), print_order_created, and ~18 ms_* events (grep of broadcastToWorkspace) onto the same /api/events stream the dashboard subscribes to.
- **Problem:** The dashboard presents itself as the live command surface, yet a payment landing, a contract getting signed, or a new booking produces no toast, no badge increment, and no refresh there — those signals are emitted into the stream and dropped. Users learn about them only by navigating into each module, which directly contradicts the 'one connected operating system' goal and makes the real-time layer feel half-wired.
- **Recommendation:** Extend the dashboard onmessage switch to handle payment_paid, cs_signed, booking_created, print_order_created (toast + badge + targeted state update) and route ms_* into the existing liveEvents/notification feed. Better: lift SSE handling into a shared useWorkspaceEvents hook consumed by every shell so all surfaces react to the same event vocabulary.

**`gap-7` — Two separate trash systems with conflicting retention windows and different shells; most modules have none**  
MED · L effort · high impact · consistency · standard: Platform-wide soft-delete / undo primitive · _completeness_  
- **Evidence:** trash/page.js (leads) states 'Trash auto-purges after 90 days' and uses NavBar shell (import line 6); studio/trash/page.js daysLeft() computes `30 - elapsed` (line 11) and uses StudioShell (import line 7). No trash/restore exists for contracts, invoices, bookings, payments, store products, galleries (grep of *API restore/trash only matches leads + media).
- **Problem:** 'Recycle bin' is not a platform primitive — it's two divergent implementations that even disagree on policy (90-day vs 30-day purge) and sit in different navigation shells, while every other destructive action (delete a contract, void an invoice, cancel a booking, delete a product) is permanent with no undo. This is precisely the 'one OS' inconsistency: the same mental model behaves differently per module and is absent elsewhere.
- **Recommendation:** Introduce one soft-delete + restore convention (deleted_at column + shared retention constant + a single /trash surface filtered by entity type) and back the leads and media trash UIs with it so retention and chrome match; extend soft-delete to contracts/invoices/bookings/products incrementally. At minimum reconcile the 90 vs 30 day windows into one documented constant.

**`gap-3` — Booking double-booking guard checks exact start time only, allowing overlapping bookings**  
HIGH · M effort · medium impact · data-integrity · standard: Scheduling slot-collision integrity · _completeness_  
- **Evidence:** booking.js:71 computeSlots free() correctly tests interval overlap [s, s+(dur+buffer)] against existing bookings, but the create guard at booking.js:143 is `SELECT id FROM bookings WHERE workspace_id=? AND start_at=? AND status!='cancelled'` (exact-match only); reschedule guard booking.js:188 is the same exact-match. Slots are generated per requested service duration but the guard ignores duration/buffer.
- **Problem:** Two bookings that overlap but don't start at the identical second both succeed — e.g. a 60-min session at 10:00 and a 30-min session at 10:30, or any two services of different durations. The slot list hides this from honest sequential users, but concurrent submissions or differing service durations cause real overbooking. The 409 'just taken' message gives false confidence. This is a calendar-integrity bug in a public, money-adjacent flow.
- **Recommendation:** Replace the exact-match guards (booking.js:143 and :188) with the same interval-overlap test computeSlots uses (compute [start, start+duration+buffer] for the requested service and reject if it intersects any non-cancelled booking), and wrap find-or-create-lead + insert in a single better-sqlite3 transaction so concurrent requests serialize. Also re-validate the slot against availability/blackout/lead-time, not just collisions.

**`gap-4` — Booking times are studio-local-naive but rendered in the viewer's timezone, and bypass the shared formatter**  
HIGH · M effort · medium impact · data-integrity · standard: Single canonical timezone handling · _completeness_  
- **Evidence:** booking.js:53 comments 'slots are studio-local' and the configured timezone field is 'display label … slots are studio-local'; start_at is stored as 'YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS' naive. bookings/page.js:127 renders it via `new Date(b.start_at.replace(' ','T')).toLocaleString(...)` — parsed in the VIEWER's browser tz. Meanwhile the shared datetime.js:31-33 explicitly appends 'Z' and treats every zone-less timestamp as UTC. The booking confirmation SMS (booking.js:166) and reminders also use new Date(start_at.replace(...)).toLocaleString() on the SERVER's tz.
- **Problem:** Three different interpretations of one timestamp: stored as studio-local, displayed in the admin's browser-local, and (in SMS/reminders) rendered in the Node server's tz. A studio in Karachi whose admin travels, or whose server runs UTC, will show/send wrong appointment times. The studio's own configured booking timezone is never actually applied. This is silent and high-consequence (missed shoots).
- **Recommendation:** Pick one canonical storage convention (recommend store UTC) and render every booking time through a single helper that formats in the studio's configured timezone (Intl with timeZone option), used by the bookings list, the public manage page, the confirmation SMS, and reminders. At minimum, route bookings/page.js:127 through the shared datetime formatter and stop the naive .replace() parsing.

**`gap-6` — Dashboard fires up to 30 per-lead requests on every load to assemble reminders**  
HIGH · M effort · medium impact · performance · standard: N+1 elimination / bounded list fetches · _completeness_  
- **Evidence:** dashboard/page.js:754-757: `leadsData.slice(0,30).map(lead => leadsAPI.getById(lead.id) ...)` then Promise.all — i.e. 30 individual GET /leads/:id round-trips on every dashboard mount purely to collect reminders. fetchLeads() (731-738) and the lead_created/missed_sync handlers also re-fetch ALL leads with leadsAPI.getAll(null) (no pagination param).
- **Problem:** Opening the dashboard issues 1 list call + analytics + tags + 30 detail calls; each detail call returns the full lead payload (messages, notes, history) just to read reminders. On a populated workspace this is dozens of requests and a visibly slow, janky first paint — the exact 'god-page / unbounded fetch' failure mode, but with a precise, fixable cause.
- **Recommendation:** Add a single GET /api/reminders?upcoming=1 (or include an upcoming_reminders array on the leads list response) so the dashboard fetches reminders in one call; stop calling getById in a loop. Make fetchLeads/getAll accept a bounded page size instead of null (fetch-all).

**`gap-8` — Invoice 'paid' has two unreconciled paths and contract-generated invoices render a colorless status**  
MED · M effort · medium impact · data-integrity · standard: Single source of truth for payment state · _completeness_  
- **Evidence:** invoices/page.js:354 marks paid via `invoicesAPI.update(id,{status:'paid'})` — creates NO payments row. payments.js settle() (65) flips the invoice to paid when a payment link is paid — creates no back-link to the invoice. Contract automation inserts invoices with status 'sent' (contracts-studio.js:290), but invoices/page.js:37 statusColors only defines paid/pending/overdue/draft — 'sent' has no color.
- **Problem:** Revenue can be recorded two ways that don't agree: a directly-marked-paid invoice has no entry in the payments ledger (so any payments-based report under-counts), and a payment-settled invoice has no link recorded on the invoice. Separately, contract-born invoices ('sent') show a blank/undefined status badge in the list. Both erode trust in the money rail and the invoice list's visual consistency.
- **Recommendation:** Route invoice 'mark paid' through the payments rail (create a manual payment record that settles the invoice) so there is one ledger, or have settle() insert a reconciling payment row; and add 'sent' (and any real 'overdue') to invoices/page.js statusColors so every backend-produced status renders consistently.

**`gap-9` — Client gallery password is sent as a URL query parameter**  
MED · S effort · low impact · standards · standard: Secrets must not travel in URLs · _completeness_  
- **Evidence:** g/[token]/page.js:47 builds `${BASE_URL}/api/media/portal/${token}?pw=${encodeURIComponent(password)}` and fetches it via GET. The password therefore appears in the URL.
- **Problem:** Gallery passwords land in browser history, server access logs, any reverse-proxy logs, and the Referer header of outbound requests from the gallery page — a low-but-real credential-leak path on a public, client-facing surface. It's also inconsistent with the other public flows which POST credentials/selections in the body.
- **Recommendation:** Send the gallery password in a POST body (or an Authorization-style header) to the portal endpoint instead of the query string, mirroring how signPublicDoc/createOrder POST their payloads. Keep the token in the path; keep the secret out of the URL.

**`gap-10` — Public surface and onClick-heavy UI lack accessibility primitives**  
LOW · L effort · low impact · standards · standard: WCAG 2.1 AA basics (name/role/value, keyboard operability) · _completeness_  
- **Evidence:** Across wappflow-web/src: 1074 onClick handlers but only 19 aria-label occurrences; just 34 of 100 page/component files use any aria-/role/alt. Example: d/[token]/page.js icon-only controls (clear/close ×, line 157,220) and the AskWidget FAB (151) have no aria-label; gallery lightbox controls in g/[token] are icon buttons.
- **Problem:** Icon-only buttons (close ×, clear, prev/next, FABs) across the authenticated and public surfaces are unlabeled, and many interactive elements are likely non-button clickable divs, making the product hard to operate with a keyboard or screen reader. For a tool that sends links to external clients (galleries, contracts, payment), the public pages especially should meet a basic a11y bar.
- **Recommendation:** Add aria-label to all icon-only buttons (start with the public /d, /g, /pay, /book pages), ensure interactive elements are real <button>s with type, and add a lightweight eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y pass to the web build to prevent regressions. Treat the public client-journey pages as the priority tier.

---

## 11. Appendix — findings reviewed & dismissed

The adversarial accuracy pass examined every high-severity finding against the code and dismissed 3 as false-positives (the code already handles it, or the claim did not hold up). Listed here for transparency.

- **`command-center-10` Suspend has no actual enforcement — suspended users can still log in** — _dismissed:_ Claim is wrong. The core auth middleware DOES enforce suspension: server.js:193-200 — after resolving workspaceId, for any non-impersonated request it runs `SELECT status FROM workspaces WHERE id=?` and returns 403 {suspended:true} when status==='suspended'. Since every per-workspace API route mounts `auth`, a suspended workspace's users are blocked from all API access on their next request (impersonating platform admins intentionally bypass so support can investigate). The finding's evidence 'No auth/login path checks workspace.status' is factually incorrect. What IS true: the suspend handler's response note (command-center.js:398) is stale/apologetic and there is no proactive forced-logout broadcast (the lockout takes effect on the next request, not instantly). That is a copy/polish nit, not the claimed 'suspension is cosmetic / users operate normally' correctness gap. Recommend re-filing as a low-severity 'remove stale note + optionally broadcast forced logout' item.
- **`discoverability-1` t** — _dismissed:_ This is a placeholder/stub entry, not a real finding: title='t', evidence='e', problem='p', recommendation='r'. There is no verifiable claim and no concrete file/line evidence to check. Cannot be confirmed; should be dropped.
- **`standards-1` Test finding** — _dismissed:_ Placeholder/stub entry: title='Test finding', problem='Test problem.', recommendation='Test recommendation.' The only concrete reference is 'NavBar.js line 712', which I checked — it contains `<AICommandCenter enabled={true} />` (a legitimate component mount), but the finding describes no actual issue with it. There is no real, actionable claim to verify. Should be dropped.

### Areas the audit itself flagged as under-covered (candidates for a deeper second pass)
- Public client journey BRANDING divergence — the survey flagged 'unbranded fragmented public journey' as a theme but did not enumerate that the 6 public pages each brand differently (contract /d shows a hardcoded WappFlow 'W'; gallery /g shows a 'Delivered with WappFlow Media Studio' footer; /client, /shop, /book show studio brand + first-letter avatar + 'Powered by'; /pay shows no brand at all), nor that company_logo (uploaded via /api/settings/logo, stored in company_settings.company_logo) is never rendered on ANY public page.
- App Router resilience standards — no audit of error.js / not-found.js / loading.js boundaries (there are ZERO across all 80+ routes), which is a concrete platform-wide standards gap distinct from the design-system theme.
- Accessibility / a11y standard — only 19 aria-labels and 34 of 100 files use any aria/role/alt against 1074 onClick handlers; the standards-matrix appears not to have scored keyboard/screen-reader support.
- Timezone correctness across modules — booking start_at is stored studio-local-naive while the shared datetime.js treats all zone-less timestamps as UTC; the bookings list bypasses the shared formatter entirely. This data-integrity class was not covered.
- Booking overbooking / slot-integrity logic — computeSlots uses interval-overlap with duration+buffer but the actual insert guard only checks exact start_at equality, allowing overlapping bookings of differing durations; not covered by the generic money/auth security spine.
- Invoice status lifecycle & ledger reconciliation — two divergent paths to 'paid' (direct invoice.update vs payments.settle) that don't reconcile, plus a 'sent' status with no color in the frontend map; under-covered relative to the broader money-rail theme.
- Dashboard real-time event coverage — the main dashboard SSE switch silently drops payment_paid, cs_signed, booking_created, print_order_created and all ms_* events; the SSE theme covered naming/transport, not which emitted events have no consumer.
